      "usmleCategory": "Neoplasms of Breast & Reproductive Tract",
      "usmleSubtopic": "Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology",
      "question": "<p>A 52-year-old woman has felt a lump in her breast for the past 2 months. On physical examination a firm 2 to 3 cm mass is palpable in the upper outer quadrant of her right breast. There are no palpable axillary nodes. A lumpectomy with axillary node dissection is performed. The breast lesion is found to have positive immunohistochemical staining for HER2/neu (c-erb B2). Staining for estrogen and progesterone receptors is negative. Which of the following treatment options is most likely to be efficacious in this woman?</p>",
      "options": [
        "Letrozole",
        "Tamoxifen",
        "Trastuzumab",
        "Prednisone",
        "Doxorubicin"
      ],
      "correct": 2,
      "correctIndex": 2,
      "explanations": [
        "Incorrect. Letrozole is an aromatase inhibitor that may be useful when the estrogen receptor assay is positive.",
        "Incorrect. Tamoxifen is most useful with estrogen receptor positive carcinomas of the breast.",
        "CORRECT. This is most likely an invasive breast carcinoma of no special type (NST), formerly calledinfiltrating ductal carcinoma. The lack of ER staining suggests a poor response to hormonal therapy with tamoxifen or letrozole. The positive C-erb B2 (HER2/neu, an epithelial growth factor receptor) staining suggests that the monoclonal antibody traztuzumab may be effective. Radical mastectomies are rarely performed.",
        "Incorrect. Prednisone is a corticosteroid that may make the patient feel better for a short time, but has no significant anti-cancer effect.",
        "Incorrect. Doxorubicin is a chemotherapy agent that may be part of regimens used to treat carcinomas, but its efficacy is not predicted by HER2 status. Some ER+ carcinomas may do worse with doxorubicin."
      ],
      "rationale": "<p><strong>Correct Answer: C. Trastuzumab</strong></p><p>CORRECT. This is most likely an invasive breast carcinoma of no special type (NST), formerly calledinfiltrating ductal carcinoma. The lack of ER staining suggests a poor response to hormonal therapy with tamoxifen or letrozole. The positive C-erb B2 (HER2/neu, an epithelial growth factor receptor) staining suggests that the monoclonal antibody traztuzumab may be effective. Radical mastectomies are rarely performed.</p>",
      "learningObjective": "This is most likely an invasive breast carcinoma of no special type (NST), formerly calledinfiltrating ductal carcinoma. The lack of ER staining suggests a poor response to hormonal therapy with tamoxifen or letrozole.",

        "CORRECT. Galactocele occurs secondary to obstruction of lactiferous ducts. It is a non-neoplastic process. Following cessation of lactation milk may be retained and produce a cystic lesion. Aspiration of the cyst removes the fluid and the cyst collapses. The cyst is lined by cuboidal to flat epithelium. The cyst contents often appear milky, but microscopically contain inflammatory cells and/or necrotic debris. Extravasation of the milk may produce chronic inflammation and even fat necrosis.",
        "Incorrect. There is lobular hyperplasia post-partum, but the epithelium of ducts does not proliferate to a great degree. Any physiologic hyperplasia will be uniform throughout the breast, not producing a local mass.",
        "Incorrect. Sclerosing adenosis is part of fibrocystic changes of the breast. Any cystic changes are microscopic, and the process is often found on examination or by radiologic imaging as irregular densities."

The right testis is palpated in the scrotum and is of normal size, but a left testis cannot be palpated in the scrotum. An ultrasound scan shows that there is a 2 cm solid inguinal mass. Which of the following approaches is most appropriate to deal with this patient's testicular abnormality?</p>",
      "options": [
        "Put the mass into the scrotum surgically",
        "Remove the mass along with the opposite testis",
        "Remove the mass",
        "Put the patient on testosterone therapy",
        "Put the patient on chemotherapy"
      ],
      "correct": 2,
      "correctIndex": 2,
      "explanations": [
        "Incorrect. The testis has been out of place too long to retain any function of spermatogenesis. It is now atrophic with few remaining germ cells and minimal, if any, spermatogenesis occurring. There is a risk for carcinoma.",
        "Incorrect. The opposite testis will not be affected by cancer risk present in the cryptorchid testis. Removing the descended testis removes fertility and hormone production.",
        "CORRECT. A cryptorchid testis that is not treated in early childhood no longer functions in spermatogenesis and presents a risk for subsequent development of seminoma. The earlier in life that an orchiopexy is performed, generally under the age of 5, the more likely the testis will function properly. If the opposite testis were also cryptorchid, it would be at increased risk for development of carcinoma. However, the risk of carcinoma in the descended testis is unlikely to be increased, so it can be left to function. The patient may opt for orchiopexy of the cryptorchid testis, but it can be explained that fertility and hormone production can be provided by the descended testis. If the patient wanted two 'balls' in the scrotum, a prosthesis could be placed.",
        "Incorrect. The patient still has one good testis. Even with bilateral cryptorchidism, the Leydig cells of the testicular interstitium continue to function and produce testosterone.",
        "Incorrect. This mass is likely the undescended testis; it is unlikely to have a carcinoma, though there is increasing risk over time."
      ],
      "rationale": "<p><strong>Correct Answer: C. Remove the mass</strong></p><p>CORRECT. A cryptorchid testis that is not treated in early childhood no longer functions in spermatogenesis and presents a risk for subsequent development of seminoma. The earlier in life that an orchiopexy is performed, generally under the age of 5, the more likely the testis will function properly. If the opposite testis were also cryptorchid, it would be at increased risk for development of carcinoma. However, the risk of carcinoma in the descended testis is unlikely to be increased, so it can be left to function. The patient may opt for orchiopexy of the cryptorchid testis, but it can be explained that fertility and hormone production can be provided by the descended testis. If the patient wanted two 'balls' in the scrotum, a prosthesis could be placed.</p>",

### MODULE 1: WebPath: Breast Pathology Exam (27 Questions) (27 QUESTIONS) ###

QUESTION 1 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q1]
Category: Neoplasms of Breast & Reproductive Tract | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
Focus: Drugs & Management (Pharmacotherapy & Drug Selection)
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 52-year-old woman has felt a lump in her breast for the past 2 months. On physical examination a firm 2 to 3 cm mass is palpable in the upper outer quadrant of her right breast. There are no palpable axillary nodes. A lumpectomy with axillary node dissection is performed. The breast lesion is found to have positive immunohistochemical staining for HER2/neu (c-erb B2). Staining for estrogen and progesterone receptors is negative. Which of the following treatment options is most likely to be efficacious in this woman?

CHOICES:
  A. Letrozole
  B. Tamoxifen
  C. Trastuzumab <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  D. Prednisone
  E. Doxorubicin

CORRECT ANSWER: C. Trastuzumab

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  This is most likely an invasive breast carcinoma of no special type (NST), formerly calledinfiltrating ductal carcinoma. The lack of ER staining suggests a poor response to hormonal therapy with tamoxifen or letrozole.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: C. Trastuzumab

CORRECT. This is most likely an invasive breast carcinoma of no special type (NST), formerly calledinfiltrating ductal carcinoma. The lack of ER staining suggests a poor response to hormonal therapy with tamoxifen or letrozole. The positive C-erb B2 (HER2/neu, an epithelial growth factor receptor) staining suggests that the monoclonal antibody traztuzumab may be effective. Radical mastectomies are rarely performed.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Letrozole is an aromatase inhibitor that may be useful when the estrogen receptor assay is positive.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Tamoxifen is most useful with estrogen receptor positive carcinomas of the breast.
  Choice C: CORRECT. This is most likely an invasive breast carcinoma of no special type (NST), formerly calledinfiltrating ductal carcinoma. The lack of ER staining suggests a poor response to hormonal therapy with tamoxifen or letrozole. The positive C-erb B2 (HER2/neu, an epithelial growth factor receptor) staining suggests that the monoclonal antibody traztuzumab may be effective. Radical mastectomies are rarely performed.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Prednisone is a corticosteroid that may make the patient feel better for a short time, but has no significant anti-cancer effect.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Doxorubicin is a chemotherapy agent that may be part of regimens used to treat carcinomas, but its efficacy is not predicted by HER2 status. Some ER+ carcinomas may do worse with doxorubicin.


----------------------------------------------------------------------
QUESTION 2 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q2]
Category: Neoplasms of Breast & Reproductive Tract | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 47-year-old woman undergoes routine mammographic screening and there are multiple small areas of increased density, though a single distinctive mass lesion is not detectable either by palpation or by mammography. A fine needle aspiration biopsy of an abnormal density reveals cells suspicious for a malignancy. An excisional breast biopsy yields a diagnosis of lobular carcinoma in situ of the breast. Which of the following is the most likely finding associated with this woman's carcinoma?

CHOICES:
  A. No residual carcinoma
  B. Opposite breast involvement <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  C. Absent family history of breast cancer
  D. Concomitant Paget disease of the nipple
  E. Negative estrogen receptor assay

CORRECT ANSWER: B. Opposite breast involvement

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Bilaterality is a frequent characteristic of lobular carcinoma in situ (LCIS). The prognosis is still good, but more surgery may be needed.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: B. Opposite breast involvement

CORRECT. Bilaterality is a frequent characteristic of lobular carcinoma in situ (LCIS). The prognosis is still good, but more surgery may be needed.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. With multiple densities, a simple excision of one of them is likely not curative. Lobular carcinoma in situ (LCIS) may not be localized to a single focus, and the opposite breast may also be involved.
  Choice B: CORRECT. Bilaterality is a frequent characteristic of lobular carcinoma in situ (LCIS). The prognosis is still good, but more surgery may be needed.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Breast cancer, including LCIS, is more likely to occur with a family history.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Paget disease of the nipple is most often associated with an underlying breast carcinoma.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Most breast cancers are estrogen receptor positive, particularly the better differentiated and in situ carcinomas.


----------------------------------------------------------------------
QUESTION 3 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q3]
Category: Benign Breast Disorders | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 15-year-old healthy girl of normal height and weight for age is concerned because her right breast has developed to twice the size of her left breast since the onset of puberty at age 12. On physical examination both breasts have a similar consistency on palpation, with no lumps, and there is no tenderness. The nipples and areolae appear normally formed. Which of the following is the most likely cause for these findings?

CHOICES:
  A. Cystosarcoma phyllodes
  B. Adrenogenital syndrome
  C. Virginal hypertrophy <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  D. Fibrocystic changes
  E. Ductal carcinoma in situ

CORRECT ANSWER: C. Virginal hypertrophy

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Virginal hypertrophy, or rapid enlargement of one or both breasts following puberty, is a problem that can be easily remedied with plastic surgery, and tamoxifen therapy may reduce recurrence. As breasts are enlarging following puberty, there is often some unequal growth, but eventually an equal size is reached by the end of puberty.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: C. Virginal hypertrophy

CORRECT. Virginal hypertrophy, or rapid enlargement of one or both breasts following puberty, is a problem that can be easily remedied with plastic surgery, and tamoxifen therapy may reduce recurrence. As breasts are enlarging following puberty, there is often some unequal growth, but eventually an equal size is reached by the end of puberty.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Phyllodes tumors are large bulky tumors that typically does not appear in persons this young.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Any syndrome of hormone excess from any source would affect both breasts.
  Choice C: CORRECT. Virginal hypertrophy, or rapid enlargement of one or both breasts following puberty, is a problem that can be easily remedied with plastic surgery, and tamoxifen therapy may reduce recurrence. As breasts are enlarging following puberty, there is often some unequal growth, but eventually an equal size is reached by the end of puberty.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Fibrocystic changes are usually seen later and produce palpable breast 'lumps' but not significant breast enlargement.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Breast carcinomas are uncommon below age 25 and do not typically produce significant breast enlargement.


----------------------------------------------------------------------
QUESTION 4 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q4]
Category: Benign Breast Disorders | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 20-year-old woman notes a mass in her left breast after following the directions for breast self-examination provided by her health clinic. Her physician palpates a firm, 1 to 2 cm mass. There is no nipple discharge and no pain. No axillary adenopathy is present. The overlying skin of the breast appears normal. Her left breast is slightly larger than the right, a condition she says has been present since puberty. Her urine pregnancy test is negative. Mammography confirms the presence of a rounded density, which has no microcalcifications, and reveals no lesions of the opposite breast. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Focus of fat necrosis
  B. Fibroadenoma <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  C. Intraductal papilloma
  D. Invasive breast carcinoma, NST
  E. Phyllodes tumor

CORRECT ANSWER: B. Fibroadenoma

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  At her age, such a firm small movable mass is most likely to be a benign lesion, and a fibroadenoma is the most likely cause for this small 'lump' in the breast. Cancers are not common at this age.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: B. Fibroadenoma

CORRECT. At her age, such a firm small movable mass is most likely to be a benign lesion, and a fibroadenoma is the most likely cause for this small 'lump' in the breast. Cancers are not common at this age.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Fat necrosis follows trauma to the breast, and though producing a mass lesion, it is not discrete, and may mimic carcinoma.
  Choice B: CORRECT. At her age, such a firm small movable mass is most likely to be a benign lesion, and a fibroadenoma is the most likely cause for this small 'lump' in the breast. Cancers are not common at this age.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Large duct papillomas are not common. They tend to be subareolar, and they tend to be associated with bloody nipple discharge.
  Choice D: Incorrect. A carcinoma would not be expected to be discrete, and age 25 is quite young for carcinoma.
  Choice E: Incorrect. A phyllodes tumor is typically a large mass. It is far less common than fibroadenoma.


----------------------------------------------------------------------
QUESTION 5 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q5]
Category: Benign Breast Disorders | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 19-year-old woman gave birth to a healthy male infant at term following an uncomplicated pregnancy. She has now been breast feeding the baby for a month, but notes that her left breast has gradually become swollen and painful to touch over the past week. On physical examination her temperature is 38.2°C. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Acute mastitis <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  B. Fibrocystic disease
  C. Fat necrosis
  D. Intraductal papilloma
  E. Galactocele

CORRECT ANSWER: A. Acute mastitis

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  The causative organism is most often <em>Staphylococcus aureus</em> or <em>Streptococcus</em>. Antibiotic therapy is usually successful.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: A. Acute mastitis

CORRECT. The causative organism is most often Staphylococcus aureus or Streptococcus. Antibiotic therapy is usually successful.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: CORRECT. The causative organism is most often <em>Staphylococcus aureus</em> or <em>Streptococcus</em>. Antibiotic therapy is usually successful.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Fibrocystic changes lead to breast lumps but not to acute inflammation.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Fat necrosis is typically the result of trauma and may produce a palpable mass lesion, but not acute inflammatory changes.
  Choice D: Incorrect. An intraductal papilloma may produce a bloody nipple discharge, but not acute inflammation.
  Choice E: Incorrect. A galactocele could arise with lactation, but acute inflammation is not part of the picture.


----------------------------------------------------------------------
QUESTION 6 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q6]
Category: Benign Breast Disorders | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 35-year-old woman has noted a palpably firm, irregular mass in her right breast for the past 3 months. On physical examination there is no tenderness or swelling. By mammography there is an irregular 2 cm density that contains scattered microcalcifications. Biopsy of this mass reveals extensive fat necrosis. Which of the following is the most likely cause for this breast lesion?

CHOICES:
  A. Pregnancy
  B. Prolactinoma
  C. Trauma <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  D. Fibrocystic changes
  E. Lobular carcinoma in situ
  F. Mastitis

CORRECT ANSWER: C. Trauma

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Trauma leads to fat necrosis. This lesion can sometimes mimic a carcinoma on physical examination and on the grossly excised breast tissue.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: C. Trauma

CORRECT. Trauma leads to fat necrosis. This lesion can sometimes mimic a carcinoma on physical examination and on the grossly excised breast tissue.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Pregnancy is associated with lactational changes, but fat necrosis is not part of this process.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Galactorrhea may complicate a pituitary adenoma secreting prolactin.
  Choice C: CORRECT. Trauma leads to fat necrosis. This lesion can sometimes mimic a carcinoma on physical examination and on the grossly excised breast tissue.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Fibrocystic changes may include cysts, duct enlargement, and fibrous thickening, but necrosis is not part of this process.
  Choice E: Incorrect. LCIS may not be palpable, or produce subtle irregular densities, because the foci are small, and necrosis is typically not part of the process.
  Choice F: Incorrect. Mastitis is more likely to present as a more diffuse process, associated with pain and tenderness. Often mastitis is associated with breast feeding.


----------------------------------------------------------------------
QUESTION 7 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q7]
Category: Benign Breast Disorders | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 30-year-old postpartum woman notes a palpable 'lump' in one of her breasts. The lump appeared following cessation of breast feeding and has persisted for more than one month. On examination there is a 1.5 cm movable nodule in her right breast. There is no axillary lymphadenopathy. Fine needle aspiration is performed and fluid is obtained. The lesion resolves and does not recur. Which of the following breast lesions is most likely to fulfill these criteria?

CHOICES:
  A. Papilloma
  B. Fat necrosis
  C. Galactocele <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  D. Ductal epithelial hyperplasia
  E. Sclerosing adenosis

CORRECT ANSWER: C. Galactocele

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Galactocele occurs secondary to obstruction of lactiferous ducts. It is a non-neoplastic process.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: C. Galactocele

CORRECT. Galactocele occurs secondary to obstruction of lactiferous ducts. It is a non-neoplastic process. Following cessation of lactation milk may be retained and produce a cystic lesion. Aspiration of the cyst removes the fluid and the cyst collapses. The cyst is lined by cuboidal to flat epithelium. The cyst contents often appear milky, but microscopically contain inflammatory cells and/or necrotic debris. Extravasation of the milk may produce chronic inflammation and even fat necrosis.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Papillomas are solid masses, not related to pregnancy. They may be associated with a nipple discharge, but the fluid may be bloody and not resemble milk.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Fat necrosis is typically produced by trauma to the breast. The lesion is solid.
  Choice C: CORRECT. Galactocele occurs secondary to obstruction of lactiferous ducts. It is a non-neoplastic process. Following cessation of lactation milk may be retained and produce a cystic lesion. Aspiration of the cyst removes the fluid and the cyst collapses. The cyst is lined by cuboidal to flat epithelium. The cyst contents often appear milky, but microscopically contain inflammatory cells and/or necrotic debris. Extravasation of the milk may produce chronic inflammation and even fat necrosis.
  Choice D: Incorrect. There is lobular hyperplasia post-partum, but the epithelium of ducts does not proliferate to a great degree. Any physiologic hyperplasia will be uniform throughout the breast, not producing a local mass.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Sclerosing adenosis is part of fibrocystic changes of the breast. Any cystic changes are microscopic, and the process is often found on examination or by radiologic imaging as irregular densities.


----------------------------------------------------------------------
QUESTION 8 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q8]
Category: Benign Breast Disorders | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 49-year-old woman notes increasing size to her right breast over the past year. This breast is not painful, but the heaviness causes some discomfort. On physical examination the overlying skin and nipple appear normal. There is no nipple discharge. There is no axillary lymphadenopathy. Mammography reveals a solid 12-cm circumscribed mass. The mass is biopsied, and on microscopic examination shows a cellular stromal component along with an epithelial component. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Fibroadenoma
  B. Phyllodes tumor <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  C. Sclerosing adenosis
  D. Hamartoma
  E. Medullary carcinoma

CORRECT ANSWER: B. Phyllodes tumor

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Phyllodes tumors are much less common than fibroadenomas, but they can be quite large. The stromal component of a phyllodes tumor is more cellular than a fibroadenoma, and it may show atypia or frank malignancy.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: B. Phyllodes tumor

CORRECT. Phyllodes tumors are much less common than fibroadenomas, but they can be quite large. The stromal component of a phyllodes tumor is more cellular than a fibroadenoma, and it may show atypia or frank malignancy.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Fibroadenomas have a stromal and epithelial component, but are not highly cellular, and they are usually small.
  Choice B: CORRECT. Phyllodes tumors are much less common than fibroadenomas, but they can be quite large. The stromal component of a phyllodes tumor is more cellular than a fibroadenoma, and it may show atypia or frank malignancy.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Sclerosing adenosis is part of fibrocystic changes and does not form a large mass, though it may produce a palpable lump or a radiologic imaging density.
  Choice D: Incorrect. A hamartoma is a solid mass lesion that is quite rare in the breast.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Medullary carcinomas can be bulky tumors, but there is no stromal component.

QUESTION 9 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q9]
Category: Neoplasms of Breast & Reproductive Tract | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
Focus: Drugs & Management (Pharmacotherapy & Drug Selection)
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 46-year-old woman has a silicone breast implant placed following a left mastectomy for treatment of an invasive breast carcinoma. She is advised of potential complications, including leakage of the silicone breast implant. Which of the following is most likely to be caused by breast implant leakage?

CHOICES:
  A. Phyllodes tumor
  B. Scleroderma
  C. Breast abscess
  D. Invasive breast carcinoma, NST
  E. Contracture <-- CORRECT ANSWER

CORRECT ANSWER: E. Contracture

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Pain and contracture with cosmetic deformity are the major complications of leaking breast implants.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: E. Contracture

CORRECT. Pain and contracture with cosmetic deformity are the major complications of leaking breast implants.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. This is a rare tumor and is not associated with breast implants.
  Choice B: Incorrect. There is no convincing evidence that immunologic disease results from breast implants.
  Choice C: Incorrect. The implant within the breast is unlikely to become infected, ruptured or not.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Women with breast implants actually have a lower rate of carcinoma of the breast. In women who have had breast cancer, the risk of recurrence is not increased.
  Choice E: CORRECT. Pain and contracture with cosmetic deformity are the major complications of leaking breast implants.

QUESTION 10 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q10]
Category: Neoplasms of Breast & Reproductive Tract | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 52-year-old woman feels a lump in her right breast on self-examination and goes to her physician. On physical examination the 4 cm mass is not freely movable and feels quite hard. A fine needle aspirate is performed and cytologic examination shows cells are present consistent with carcinoma. Which of the following features of this carcinoma is most likely to suggest a worse prognosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Estrogen receptor positivity
  B. Family history of breast carcinoma
  C. Presence of an in-situ component
  D. Axillary lymph node metastases <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  E. Lack of aneuploidy

CORRECT ANSWER: D. Axillary lymph node metastases

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Invasion of lymphatics is a poor prognostic sign, because it suggests that the carcinoma is spreading.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: D. Axillary lymph node metastases

CORRECT. Invasion of lymphatics is a poor prognostic sign, because it suggests that the carcinoma is spreading.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. An ER postitive tumor has a better prognosis because it will respond to certain hormonal therapies.
  Choice B: Incorrect. A family history makes breast cancer more likely, but does not predict prognosis.
  Choice C: Incorrect. In situ lesions are the lowest stage possible for a carcinoma.
  Choice D: CORRECT. Invasion of lymphatics is a poor prognostic sign, because it suggests that the carcinoma is spreading.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Aneuploidy diagnosed with flow cytometry suggests a worse prognosis.

QUESTION 11 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q11]
Category: Neoplasms of Breast & Reproductive Tract | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 45-year-old woman feels a 'lump' in her left breast. Her nurse practitioner palpates a 2 cm irregular area in the upper outer quadrant. A biopsy is performed and microscopic examination shows no evidence for carcinoma. Which of the following microscopic findings in this biopsy is most likely to suggest an increased risk for subsequent development of breast carcinoma?

CHOICES:
  A. Atypical hyperplasia <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  B. Sclerosing adenosis
  C. Apocrine metaplasia
  D. Galactocele
  E. Multiple cystic spaces

CORRECT ANSWER: A. Atypical hyperplasia

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Particularly when atypical microscopic features are present, lobular or ductal hyperplasia is the component of fibrocystic disease that is associated with subsequent increased risk for development of carcinoma, probably about a 5 fold risk.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: A. Atypical hyperplasia

CORRECT. Particularly when atypical microscopic features are present, lobular or ductal hyperplasia is the component of fibrocystic disease that is associated with subsequent increased risk for development of carcinoma, probably about a 5 fold risk.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: CORRECT. Particularly when atypical microscopic features are present, lobular or ductal hyperplasia is the component of fibrocystic disease that is associated with subsequent increased risk for development of carcinoma, probably about a 5 fold risk.
  Choice B: Incorrect. This component of fibrocystic disease has about a 2 fold increased risk for breast cancer.
  Choice C: Incorrect. This lesion may be part of fibrocystic changes, but it does not predispose to carcinoma.
  Choice D: Incorrect. This is a complication of obstruction to lactiferous sinuses during lactation. It may rarely occur with oral contraceptive use. It is not premalignant.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Simple cysts with fibrocystic disease are not associated with an increased risk for carcinoma.

QUESTION 12 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q12]
Category: Benign Breast Disorders | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 20-year-old woman gives birth to a term girl infant following an uncomplicated pregnancy. She breast feeds the infant. Six weeks later, her left breast becomes painful and slightly swollen. On physical examination there is a tender 3 cm mass in the left breast beneath a nipple that shows several painful fissures. Which of the following pathologic findings is most likely to be present in this breast?

CHOICES:
  A. Invasive breast carcinoma, NST
  B. Plasma cell infiltrate
  C. Sclerosing adenosis
  D. <em>Staphylococcus aureus</em> infection <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  E. Fat necrosis

CORRECT ANSWER: D. <em>Staphylococcus aureus</em> infection

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  A breast abscess is present. These are most common in the postpartum period when the nipple becomes cracked or fissured with nursing.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: D. Staphylococcus aureus infection

CORRECT. A breast abscess is present. These are most common in the postpartum period when the nipple becomes cracked or fissured with nursing.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Invasive breast carcinomas of no special type (NST), formerly called ductal caracinoma, are ill-defined masses that are usually not tender. They are extremely rare before age 25.
  Choice B: Incorrect. A 'plasma cell mastitis' is the result of inspissated duct secretions and is most often seen in women in their 40's and 50's.
  Choice C: Incorrect. This is a part of fibrocystic disease, which may have a cystic mass that may be tender, but not nipple fissuring.
  Choice D: CORRECT. A breast abscess is present. These are most common in the postpartum period when the nipple becomes cracked or fissured with nursing.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Fat necrosis is usually due to trauma and leads to an ill-defined mass.

QUESTION 13 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q13]
Category: Benign Breast Disorders | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 25-year-old woman palpates a left breast 'lump' on self-examination. Her physician's assistant palpates an ill-defined mass. There is no pain or tenderness. No axillary lymphadenopathy is noted. Fine needle aspiration is performed and cytologic examination shows cells that appear benign. The lesion persists, and 6 months later another biopsy is taken and shows ductal epithelial proliferation with ductal apocrine metaplasia, stromal fibrosis, and sclerosing adenosis. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Invasive breast carcinoma, NST
  B. Ductal carcinoma in situ
  C. Lobular carcinoma in situ
  D. Fibrocystic changes <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  E. Intraductal papilloma

CORRECT ANSWER: D. Fibrocystic changes

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Fibrocystic changes are very common and benign, but can produce a mass effect and must be distinguished from breast cancer.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: D. Fibrocystic changes

CORRECT. Fibrocystic changes are very common and benign, but can produce a mass effect and must be distinguished from breast cancer.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Carcinomas have abnormal cells, not just proliferation of cells.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Ductal carcinoma in situ is marked by highly atypical, disorganized cells within ductal lumens.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Lobular carcinoma in situ is marked by highly atypical cells filling and expanding lobules.
  Choice D: CORRECT. Fibrocystic changes are very common and benign, but can produce a mass effect and must be distinguished from breast cancer.
  Choice E: Incorrect. An intraductal papilloma is a focal lesion with both epithelial and stromal proliferation in a papillary pattern.

QUESTION 14 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q14]
Category: Benign Breast Disorders | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 39-year-old woman has noted red, scaling area on her breast for 3 months. On physical examination there is an eczematous 1 cm diameter area on the skin of the right breast areola. There is no palpable lump in this breast. Biopsy of the skin lesion is performed and on microscopic examination shows large cells at the dermal-epidermal junction that stain positively for mucin. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Nipple discharge
  B. Paget disease of breast <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  C. Intraductal carcinoma
  D. Dermatophyte infection
  E. Inflammatory carcinoma
  F. Eczematous dermatitis

CORRECT ANSWER: B. Paget disease of breast

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Paget disease of the breast is a skin lesion that appears grossly eczematous, but microscopically shows the presence of malignant cells. There is typically an underlying carcinoma that may be hard to locate.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: B. Paget disease of breast

CORRECT. Paget disease of the breast is a skin lesion that appears grossly eczematous, but microscopically shows the presence of malignant cells. There is typically an underlying carcinoma that may be hard to locate.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Discharges are more likely with intraductal papillomas, and they do not lead to the appearance of malignant cells at the dermal-epidermal junction.
  Choice B: CORRECT. Paget disease of the breast is a skin lesion that appears grossly eczematous, but microscopically shows the presence of malignant cells. There is typically an underlying carcinoma that may be hard to locate.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Such a lesion should be within the breast and not involve overlying skin.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Skin infections may produce erythematous lesions, but without malignant cells present.
  Choice E: Incorrect. An 'inflammatory carcinoma' is not a histologic type of breast cancer, but a term that describes dermal lymphatic involvement by breast cancer. In this case, the malignant cells are not in lymphatics.
  Choice F: Incorrect. A rough, red, scaling patch of skin can be termed eczema, but should not contain malignant cells staining for mucin.

QUESTION 15 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q15]
Category: Benign Breast Disorders | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 39-year-old woman has noted a bloody nipple discharge from the right breast for the past 5 weeks. On physical examination there is no palpable mass or tenderness. The skin of this breast shows no lesions. A small amount of bloody fluid can be expressed from the right nipple. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Mastitis
  B. Intraductal papilloma <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  C. Trauma
  D. Invasive breast carcinoma, NST
  E. Tuberculosis
  F. Fibrocystic changes

CORRECT ANSWER: B. Intraductal papilloma

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Papilloma, a solitary lesion of a lactiferous sinus, is not common, but you should suspect it when there is a bloody discharge. This lesion is not premalignant.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: B. Intraductal papilloma

CORRECT. Papilloma, a solitary lesion of a lactiferous sinus, is not common, but you should suspect it when there is a bloody discharge. This lesion is not premalignant.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. The inflammation with mastitis does not usually erode breast tissue and cause bleeding.
  Choice B: CORRECT. Papilloma, a solitary lesion of a lactiferous sinus, is not common, but you should suspect it when there is a bloody discharge. This lesion is not premalignant.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Fat necrosis leads to scarring and hemorrhage is not common (at least, hemorrhage into the lactiferous sinuses is not common).
  Choice D: Incorrect. The slowly infiltrative growth of invasive cancers leads to scarring, not hemorrhage.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Mycobacterial infections involving the breast are quite rare.
  Choice F: Incorrect. Fibrocystic changes are not likely to be accompanied by hemorrhage.

QUESTION 16 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q16]
Category: Benign Breast Disorders | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 61-year-old woman has noted a rough, reddened appearance increasing in size over the skin of her right breast for the past 5 months. This persists despite application of a corticosteroid cream. On physical examination the skin over the right breast is indurated, roughened, and reddish-orange. There is nipple retraction. A firm, irregular 5 cm mass is palpable in this breast. Which of the following is the most likely risk factor for this woman's condition?

CHOICES:
  A. Cigarette smoking
  B. Prior fibrocystic disease
  C. Multiparity
  D. Family history <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  E. Human papillomavirus infection

CORRECT ANSWER: D. Family history

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  A The findings point to breast carcinoma with invasion of the overlying skin ('inflammatory carcinoma'). A family history of breast cancer increases the risk for breast cancer the most.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: D. Family history

CORRECT. A The findings point to breast carcinoma with invasion of the overlying skin ('inflammatory carcinoma'). A family history of breast cancer increases the risk for breast cancer the most.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Smoking increases the risk for lung cancers, urinary tract cancers, and pancreatic carcinomas. However, there is no link to breast cancers.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Fibrocystic changes in the breast are not a significant risk factor for subsequent development of breast cancer, unless there is some epithelial atypia present.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Multiparity tends to reduce the overall risk for breast cancer.
  Choice D: CORRECT. A The findings point to breast carcinoma with invasion of the overlying skin ('inflammatory carcinoma'). A family history of breast cancer increases the risk for breast cancer the most.
  Choice E: Incorrect. HPV infections increase the risk for squamous epithelial dysplasias and carcinomas in the female genital tract, not the breast.

QUESTION 17 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q17]
Category: Benign Breast Disorders | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 41-year-old woman has felt a large breast lump that has increased slowly in size for the past 3 years. On physical examination there is a 9 cm firm, movable mass present in her left breast. The overlying skin appears normal; there is no axillary lymphadenopathy. The mass is excised and grossly appears soft and fleshy. On microscopic examination this mass has a lymphoid stroma with little fibrosis surrounding sheets of large vesicular cells with frequent mitoses. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Invasive breast carcinoma, NST
  B. Colloid carcinoma
  C. Medullary carcinoma <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  D. Lobular carcinoma
  E. Phyllodes tumor
  F. Diffuse large B cell lymphoma

CORRECT ANSWER: C. Medullary carcinoma

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Medullary carcinoma is often bulky, with pushing margins. It lacks the desmoplasia typical of invasive carcinomas.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: C. Medullary carcinoma

CORRECT. Medullary carcinoma is often bulky, with pushing margins. It lacks the desmoplasia typical of invasive carcinomas. It accounts for only about 1% of breast cancers.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. An invasive breast carcinoma of no special type (NST), formerly called an invasive ductal carcinoma, is unlikely to reach this size, is irregular in shape, and it does not have a lymphoid stroma. It often has pronounced desmoplasia.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Colloid carcinomas produce abundant mucin. They are often well circumscribed.
  Choice C: CORRECT. Medullary carcinoma is often bulky, with pushing margins. It lacks the desmoplasia typical of invasive carcinomas. It accounts for only about 1% of breast cancers.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Lobular carcinomas do not form a large mass and are poorly circumscribed. They are bilateral about 20% of the time. They make up only 5 to 10% of breast cancers.
  Choice E: Incorrect. This tumor can become quite large, but is composed of both epithelial and stromal components, either or both of which may be malignant.
  Choice F: Incorrect. Non-Hodgkin lymphoma involving the breast is not common. Lymphomas do not have a malignant epithelial component.

QUESTION 18 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q18]
Category: Benign Breast Disorders | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A clinical study is performed involving subjects who are women ranging in age from 15 to 45 years who palpated breast 'lumps' on self-examination. The presence of breast mass lesions in these subjects was subsequently confirmed by physical examination and by mammography. All subjects had a needle biopsy or excision of their lesion performed, with a definitive pathologic diagnosis made. Which of the following diagnoses is likely to be the most frequently made in these subjects?

CHOICES:
  A. Abscess
  B. Fibroadenoma
  C. Fibrocystic changes <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  D. Lobular carcinoma in situ
  E. Invasive breast carcinoma, NST

CORRECT ANSWER: C. Fibrocystic changes

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  About 40% of breast lumps turn out to be caused by fibrocystic changes.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: C. Fibrocystic changes

CORRECT. About 40% of breast lumps turn out to be caused by fibrocystic changes.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Abscesses are uncommon, though some may arise as complications in women who nurse their babies and develop fissures in the nipple.
  Choice B: Incorrect. These represent about 1 in 13 lumps, but they are more prominent in younger women of reproductive years.
  Choice C: CORRECT. About 40% of breast lumps turn out to be caused by fibrocystic changes.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Lobular CIS is uncommon.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Overall, carcinomas represent about 10% of breast lumps, but it is very important to find them.

QUESTION 19 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q19]
Category: Neoplasms of Breast & Reproductive Tract | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 61-year-old woman has increasing dyspnea with chest pain for the past 4 months. On physical examination there is dullness to percussion over both lungs. A chest x-ray shows bilateral pleural effusions. A thoracentesis yields bloody fluid that cytologically contains small malignant cells forming long rows or rings. A mammogram shows an irregular area of architectural disortion in her left breast. Which of the following histologic types of breast cancer is most likely to be present in this woman?

CHOICES:
  A. Lobular <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  B. Ductal
  C. Medullary
  D. Tubular
  E. Papillary

CORRECT ANSWER: A. Lobular

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Invasive lobular carcinomas are most likely to metastasize widely to surfaces: pleural, peritoneal, meningeal. They also may metastasize to femal genital tract and gastrointestinal tract.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: A. Lobular

CORRECT. Invasive lobular carcinomas are most likely to metastasize widely to surfaces: pleural, peritoneal, meningeal. They also may metastasize to femal genital tract and gastrointestinal tract. The cells have minimal associated desmoplasia, so a palpable mass may not be present.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: CORRECT. Invasive lobular carcinomas are most likely to metastasize widely to surfaces: pleural, peritoneal, meningeal. They also may metastasize to femal genital tract and gastrointestinal tract. The cells have minimal associated desmoplasia, so a palpable mass may not be present.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Invasive carcinomas are more likely to produce a palpable mass and to metastasize first to axillary nodes.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Medullary carcinomas tend to form a bulky mass; there is often an accompanying lymphoplasmacytic component.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Tubular carcinomas form - well - tubules, and tend to be well-differentiated and less aggressive.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Papillary carcinomas are rare; they form papillations; overall they are less aggressive breast cancers.

QUESTION 20 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q20]
Category: Neoplasms of Breast & Reproductive Tract | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 30-year-old woman has felt a mass in her right breast for 6 months. On physical examination there is a 5 cm right breast mass and firm, non-tender lymphadenopathy. A fine needle aspiration is performed and cytologic examination shows carcinoma cells. A right mastectomy with axillary lymph node dissection is performed. Microscopic examination shows a poorly differentiated carcinoma that is negative for estrogen and progesterone receptors and negative for HER2/neu. One axillary lymph node demonstrates micrometastases. Her 32-year-old sister is found to have a similar lesion. Which of the following is the most likely risk factor for this woman's breast cancer?

CHOICES:
  A. <em>BRCA1</em> gene mutation <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  B. Late menarche
  C. Positive antinuclear antibody test
  D. Exposure to hydrocarbon compounds
  E. Prior fibrocystic changes

CORRECT ANSWER: A. <em>BRCA1</em> gene mutation

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  A small number of breast cancers are the result of an inherited <em>BRCA1</em> mutation (or <em>BRCA-2</em>), but the family history of breast cancer at a young age makes this more likely. The 'triple negative' breast cancers (ER, PR, HER2 negative) are harder to treat, but there are chemotherapy protocols that can be employed.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: A. BRCA1 gene mutation

CORRECT. A small number of breast cancers are the result of an inherited BRCA1 mutation (or BRCA-2), but the family history of breast cancer at a young age makes this more likely. The 'triple negative' breast cancers (ER, PR, HER2 negative) are harder to treat, but there are chemotherapy protocols that can be employed.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: CORRECT. A small number of breast cancers are the result of an inherited <em>BRCA1</em> mutation (or <em>BRCA-2</em>), but the family history of breast cancer at a young age makes this more likely. The 'triple negative' breast cancers (ER, PR, HER2 negative) are harder to treat, but there are chemotherapy protocols that can be employed.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Early menarche and late menopause and nulliparity are risks for breast cancer.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Autoimmune diseases do not appreciably increase the risk for breast cancer.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Breast cancers are not typically thought to be related to environmental exposures.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Fibrocystic changes do not substantially increase the risk for breast cancer. Atypical epithelial hyperplasia would increase the risk.

QUESTION 21 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q21]
Category: Neoplasms of Breast & Reproductive Tract | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 45-year-old woman has noted a mass in her right breast for 7 months. On physical examination there is a 5 cm diameter right breast mass. A biopsy of the mass is performed and on microscopic examination a carcinoma is present. Which of the following pathologic findings involving this lesion suggests a better prognosis for this woman?

CHOICES:
  A. Estrogen receptor negative
  B. Axillary lymph node metastasis
  C. Age at diagnosis
  D. Aneuploidy by flow cytometry
  E. Intraductal histologic pattern <-- CORRECT ANSWER

CORRECT ANSWER: E. Intraductal histologic pattern

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  The best overall survival is with intraductal carcinomas (those that have not invaded out from the ducts); this is the lowest stage possible.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: E. Intraductal histologic pattern

CORRECT. The best overall survival is with intraductal carcinomas (those that have not invaded out from the ducts); this is the lowest stage possible.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. The presence of estrogen receptors suggests better differentiation and better response to hormonal manipulation.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Axillary lymph node metastasis is the single most important prognostic factor for patients with early breast carcinoma. Metastases imply a worse prognosis.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Breast cancers occur over a wide age range, and prognosis depends more upon the type of cancer, stage, and molecular markers.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Aneuploidy suggests a more aggressive neoplasm and a worse prognosis.
  Choice E: CORRECT. The best overall survival is with intraductal carcinomas (those that have not invaded out from the ducts); this is the lowest stage possible.

QUESTION 22 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q22]
Category: Benign Breast Disorders | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 51-year-old healthy man has noted bilaterally enlarged breasts for the past 35 years. He has infertility and a prior workup showed hypospermia. On physical examination his breasts are 3 times normal size, with no masses present, no tenderness, and no axillary lymphadenopathy. Which of the following conditions is most likely to be associated with his breast enlargement?

CHOICES:
  A. Antidepressant drug therapy
  B. 47, XXY karyotype <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  C. Family history
  D. Seminoma of the testis
  E. Increased testosterone levels

CORRECT ANSWER: B. 47, XXY karyotype

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Gynecomastia is common with Klinefelter syndrome. It is a benign condition, and a relationship to development of cancer is difficult to demonstrate.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: B. 47, XXY karyotype

CORRECT. Gynecomastia is common with Klinefelter syndrome. It is a benign condition, and a relationship to development of cancer is difficult to demonstrate. Hypogonadism is another feature of Klinefelter syndrome.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Chronic alcohol use may lead to gynecomastia. Digoxin therapy may have this complication.
  Choice B: CORRECT. Gynecomastia is common with Klinefelter syndrome. It is a benign condition, and a relationship to development of cancer is difficult to demonstrate. Hypogonadism is another feature of Klinefelter syndrome.
  Choice C: Incorrect. He has a benign process. Male breast carcinomas can be associated with BRCA mutations. Male breast cancers are not common, and they more often associated with advanced age.
  Choice D: Incorrect. There is an association between gynecomastia and some functioning testicular tumors such as Leydig cell tumors (or rarely, Sertoli cell tumors).
  Choice E: Incorrect. Gynecomastia is related to hyperestrinism, and one of the most common causes for this is cirrhosis of the liver in chronic alcoholics.

QUESTION 23 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q23]
Category: Neoplasms of Breast & Reproductive Tract | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A study of breast carcinomas is conducted. Some of these carcinomas are found to be 'triple negative' for estrogen receptor, progesterone receptor, and HER2. Which of the following microscopic findings is most characteristic for these triple negative breast carcinomas?

CHOICES:
  A. Inflammatory component
  B. Multifocality
  C. High grade <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  D. Tubular growth pattern
  E. Desmoplasia

CORRECT ANSWER: C. High grade

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Triple negative breast cancers are typically high-grade, agressive cancers that tend to recur quickly and spread to visceral sites. Treatment is a challenge, because targeted hormonal therapies or biotherapies do not work well.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: C. High grade

CORRECT. Triple negative breast cancers are typically high-grade, agressive cancers that tend to recur quickly and spread to visceral sites. Treatment is a challenge, because targeted hormonal therapies or biotherapies do not work well.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. A reactive inflammatory process around the cancer does not predict prognosis. A lymphoid stroma may be a characteristic of medullary carcinomas, but does not correlate with marker status. Multiple breast cancer types may spread via lymphatics including dermal lymphatics as an 'inflammatory' carcinoma.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Some cancers, such as lobular carcinomas, may be more often multifocal, but that does not correlate with marker status.
  Choice C: CORRECT. Triple negative breast cancers are typically high-grade, agressive cancers that tend to recur quickly and spread to visceral sites. Treatment is a challenge, because targeted hormonal therapies or biotherapies do not work well.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Some breast cancers have a tubular pattern, and they tend to act in a less aggressive manner.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Desmoplasia, or production of a prominent collagenous stroma, is found in many invasive breast cancers, and imparts their firm and fixed features palpable on examination. Desmoplasia does not correlate with marker status.

QUESTION 24 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q24]
Category: Neoplasms of Breast & Reproductive Tract | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 49-year-old woman goes to her physician for a routine health maintenance examination. A screening mammogram is performed and shows a 2 cm irregular density in her left breast that has microcalcifications. A needle biopsy of this area shows ducts containing solid sheets of malignant cells, with central necrosis and calcification. There is no invasion. A lumpectomy with wide excision to clear margins is performed along with axillary lymph node sampling that shows no metastases. Malignant cells are positive for HER2 but negative for estrogen receptor. Which of the following is the most likely outcome for this woman?

CHOICES:
  A. Recurrence in the opposite breast
  B. Cure with no further therapy <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  C. Response to therapy with tamoxifen
  D. Development of inflammatory carcinoma
  E. Her children will inherit a BRCA1 mutation

CORRECT ANSWER: B. Cure with no further therapy

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  She has a ductal carcinoma in situ (DCIS). Since it is non-invasive at the time of diagnosis, it has a good prognosis.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: B. Cure with no further therapy

CORRECT. She has a ductal carcinoma in situ (DCIS). Since it is non-invasive at the time of diagnosis, it has a good prognosis. Some will recur locally. If left alone, DCIS will often progress to become an invasive carcinoma.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Appearance in the opposite breast is a feature of lobular carcinomas.
  Choice B: CORRECT. She has a ductal carcinoma in situ (DCIS). Since it is non-invasive at the time of diagnosis, it has a good prognosis. Some will recur locally. If left alone, DCIS will often progress to become an invasive carcinoma.
  Choice C: Incorrect. The lack of estrogen receptor positivity in the tumor cells predicts a poor response to hormonal therapy with tamoxifen or letrozole.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Spread via lymphatics to the skin overlying the breast is not likely, since this is an in situ carcinoma.
  Choice E: Incorrect. At her age, this is less likely to be a cancer associated with BRCA1 or BRCA-2 than in a younger woman. Only a small number of all breast cancers are associated with known mutations.

QUESTION 25 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q25]
Category: Neoplasms of Breast & Reproductive Tract | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 69-year-old woman has a screening mammogram that shows an irregular 1 to 2 cm density in her right breast. She has no axillary lymphadenopathy. A needle biopsy is performed and on microscopic examination shows malignant cells floating in mucinous lakes. The nuclei show minimal pleomorphism and no mitoses are seen. The cells are positive for estrogen receptor and negative for HER2 (c-erb B2). A chest x-ray shows no abnormal findings. A bone scan is negative. What is the most likely designation for grade and stage of this neoplasm?

CHOICES:
  A. Grade I, Stage T1 N0 M0 <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  B. Grade I, Stage T2 N1 M1
  C. Grade II, Stage T3 N0 M0
  D. Grade II, Stage T2 N1 M0
  E. Grade III, Stage T3 N1 M0

CORRECT ANSWER: A. Grade I, Stage T1 N0 M0

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  The histologic features are those of a low grade colloid carcinoma, a histologic type that typically is ER positive, without metastases, and with a good prognosis. The stage is low because there is a small tumor without evidence of either nodal or distant metastases.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: A. Grade I, Stage T1 N0 M0

CORRECT. The histologic features are those of a low grade colloid carcinoma, a histologic type that typically is ER positive, without metastases, and with a good prognosis. The stage is low because there is a small tumor without evidence of either nodal or distant metastases.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: CORRECT. The histologic features are those of a low grade colloid carcinoma, a histologic type that typically is ER positive, without metastases, and with a good prognosis. The stage is low because there is a small tumor without evidence of either nodal or distant metastases.
  Choice B: Incorrect. No evidence for nodal metastases is present; the tumor size is small.
  Choice C: Incorrect. The tumor is a low grade, and the size is small.
  Choice D: Incorrect. The tumor is a low grade, and the size is small; no evidence for nodal metastases is present.
  Choice E: Incorrect. The tumor is a low grade, and the size is small; no evidence for nodal metastases is present.

QUESTION 26 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q26]
Category: Neoplasms of Breast & Reproductive Tract | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 66-year-old man has observed an enlarging lump near the right nipple for 5 months. On examination there is a firm, fixed 1.5 cm nodule beneath the right areola. The mass is excised and axillary lymph node sampling performed. The mass and one lymph node examined microscopically show an invasive (ductal) breast carcinoma. Which of the following germline mutations is most likely present in this man?

CHOICES:
  A. <em>APC</em>
  B. <em>BRCA2</em> <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  C. <em>NF1</em>
  D. <em>TP53</em>
  E. <em>VHL</em>

CORRECT ANSWER: B. <em>BRCA2</em>

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Familial <em>BRCA2</em> mutations may be found in about 10% of male breast cancers. Other risks are the same as for women: first degree relative, exogenous extrogens, fatness, prior radiation.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: B. BRCA2

CORRECT. Familial BRCA2 mutations may be found in about 10% of male breast cancers. Other risks are the same as for women: first degree relative, exogenous extrogens, fatness, prior radiation. In addition, Klinefelter syndrome (1 in 500 men) is a risk. Most are estrogen receptor positive, and treatment strategies are the same as for women.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. <em>APC</em> gene mutations are most often associated with colonic adenocarcinomas.
  Choice B: CORRECT. Familial <em>BRCA2</em> mutations may be found in about 10% of male breast cancers. Other risks are the same as for women: first degree relative, exogenous extrogens, fatness, prior radiation. In addition, Klinefelter syndrome (1 in 500 men) is a risk. Most are estrogen receptor positive, and treatment strategies are the same as for women.
  Choice C: Incorrect. <em>NF1</em> tumor suppressor gene mutations lead to neurofibromatosis and to CNS and PNS neoplasms.
  Choice D: Incorrect. <em>TP53</em> tumor suppressor gene mutations can be found in many cancers. An inherited <em>TP53</em> mutation in the Li Fraumeni syndrome increases the risk for breast cancer. However, germline <em>TP53</em> mutation is not as common as BRCA2 in male breast cancers.
  Choice E: Incorrect. <em>VHL</em> (von Hippel Lindau) gene mutations are best known for association with renal cell carcinomas.

QUESTION 27 of 27 [ID: webpath-breast-q27]
Category: Benign Breast Disorders | Subtopic: Breast Neoplasm Diagnosis & Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 29-year-old woman notes a lump in her left breast. The lump remains through 2 menstrual cycles without change in size. A screening ultrasound examination is performed and there is a 1.5 cm fluid-filled cyst within the inner upper quadrant of the left breast. Fine needle aspiration is performed and clear fluid and some cells are obtained. A year later there is no palpable lump. Which of the following cells were most likely present in her breast lesion?

CHOICES:
  A. Atypical ductal epithelial cells
  B. Fibroblasts
  C. Lobular carcinoma cells
  D. Metaplastic apocrine cells <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  E. Neutrophils
  F. Small mature lymphocytes

CORRECT ANSWER: D. Metaplastic apocrine cells

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Breast cysts are common and part of fibrocystic changes. The cyst may be lined by flattened epithelium or larger pink cells with apocrine change.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: D. Metaplastic apocrine cells

CORRECT. Breast cysts are common and part of fibrocystic changes. The cyst may be lined by flattened epithelium or larger pink cells with apocrine change. Aspiration collapses the cyst, which may be curative.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Atypical cells suggest a pre-neoplastic process that is part of a solid mass. Such a lesion is unlikely to disappear.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Fibroblasts may be found in the stroma, not the cyst.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Lobular carcinoma would produce a solid lesion and would not regress.
  Choice D: CORRECT. Breast cysts are common and part of fibrocystic changes. The cyst may be lined by flattened epithelium or larger pink cells with apocrine change. Aspiration collapses the cyst, which may be curative.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Neutrophils would be present in an abscess with purulent fluid obtained.
  Choice F: Incorrect. Lymphocytes could be part of a lymphocytic mastopathy forming a solid lesion. There may be an autoimmune etiology.

QUESTION 1 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q1]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
Focus: Drugs & Management (Pharmacotherapy & Drug Selection)
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 29-year-old woman taking oral contraceptives for a year has noted vaginal bleeding that is not severe, but it has occurred nearly every day over the past 5 weeks. On pelvic examination, there is a 0.7 cm polypoid mass noted to extend outward from the cervical os. The ectocervix appears normal. The uterus is normal in size. The adnexa have no palpable masses. A biopsy of this lesion is performed. Which of the following microscopic findings is most likely to be found in this lesion?

CHOICES:
  A. Endocervical adenocarcinoma
  B. Clear cell adenocarcinoma
  C. Microglandular hyperplasia <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  D. Sarcoma botryoides
  E. Endocervical polyp
  F. Follicular cervicitis

CORRECT ANSWER: C. Microglandular hyperplasia

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Microglandular hyperplasia forms a mass lesion grossly, and must be distinguished from adenocarcinoma histologically. Some cases may occur in the setting of oral contraceptive use, but a causal relationship is not well established.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: C. Microglandular hyperplasia

CORRECT. Microglandular hyperplasia forms a mass lesion grossly, and must be distinguished from adenocarcinoma histologically. Some cases may occur in the setting of oral contraceptive use, but a causal relationship is not well established.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Adenocarcinomas arising in the endocervical region are uncommon and unlikely to be present at this age.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Clear cell carcinomas are uncommon lesions. They are more likely to occur in the vagina of women who were exposed to diethylstilbestrol in utero when their mothers took this drug.
  Choice C: CORRECT. Microglandular hyperplasia forms a mass lesion grossly, and must be distinguished from adenocarcinoma histologically. Some cases may occur in the setting of oral contraceptive use, but a causal relationship is not well established.
  Choice D: Incorrect. This rare tumor, a form of embryonal rhabdomyosarcoma, is seen in the vagina of young girls.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Endocervical polyps occur in about 2 to 5% of women but is not specifically related to oral contraceptive use.
  Choice F: Incorrect. Follicular cervicitis appears grossly as erythema and irregular surface features of the cervix, but not a mass.

QUESTION 2 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q2]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 48-year-old woman has noted a small amount of irregular vaginal bleeding for the past 2 months. She has a pelvic examination that reveals no cervical lesions, and a Pap smear that shows no abnormal cells. Next, an endometrial biopsy is performed, and there is microscopic evidence for endometrial hyperplasia. An abdominal ultrasound reveals a solid right ovarian mass. Which of the following neoplasms is this woman is most likely to have?

CHOICES:
  A. Mature cystic teratoma
  B. Choriocarcinoma
  C. Sex-cord stromal cell tumor
  D. Fibrothecoma <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  E. Krukenberg tumor
  F. Cystadenocarcinoma

CORRECT ANSWER: D. Fibrothecoma

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Fibrothecomas and granulosa cell tumors can be estrogen producing. The excessive estrogen drives endometrial hyperplasia, which can progress to atypical hyperplasia, which can progress to endometrial carcinoma.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: D. Fibrothecoma

CORRECT. Fibrothecomas and granulosa cell tumors can be estrogen producing. The excessive estrogen drives endometrial hyperplasia, which can progress to atypical hyperplasia, which can progress to endometrial carcinoma.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Struma ovarii is one uncommon variant of a 'dermoid' cyst that secretes significant amounts of a hormone - thyroid hormone. Such benign teratomas are invariably cystic, contain well-differentiated tissue components, and are usually filled with hair and sebum from a prominent ectodermal component.
  Choice B: Incorrect. However, theca-lutein cysts of the ovary may occur with gestational trophoblastic disease - molar pregnancy.
  Choice C: Incorrect. However, Sex-cord stromal tumors (including Leydig cell tumors and steroid cell tumors) can produce steroid hormones, ususally androgenic with virilizing symptoms.
  Choice D: CORRECT. Fibrothecomas and granulosa cell tumors can be estrogen producing. The excessive estrogen drives endometrial hyperplasia, which can progress to atypical hyperplasia, which can progress to endometrial carcinoma.
  Choice E: Incorrect. This tumor represents a metastatic adenocarcinoma (usually from a gastric primary).
  Choice F: Incorrect. Though cystadenocarcinoma is one of the more common ovarian neoplasms, it is not likely to be a source of steroid hormone secretion. Cystadenocarcinomas are likely to be cystic and not solid.

QUESTION 3 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q3]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 51-year-old perimenopausal woman has had vaginal bleeding for the past 6 months. On physical examination she has an enlarged, nodular uterus. A hysterectomy is performed. The surgeon notes several 0.2 to 1 cm translucent, smooth-surfaced, thin-walled, fluid-filled cysts near the fimbriated end of the right fallopian tube. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis for these cysts?

CHOICES:
  A. Gartner duct cysts
  B. Krukenberg tumors
  C. Parovarian cysts <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  D. Pelvic inflammatory disease
  E. Mucinous cystadenomas
  F. Fibrocystic disease
  G. Omphalomesenteric duct cysts

CORRECT ANSWER: C. Parovarian cysts

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Also called paratubal cysts, these are common incidental findings. They are embryologic remnants of the wolffian duct.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: C. Parovarian cysts

CORRECT. Also called paratubal cysts, these are common incidental findings. They are embryologic remnants of the wolffian duct. They are not related to neoplasia. This woman had leiomyomas of the uterus which led to the hysterectomy.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Such cysts are found in the anterolateral vaginal wall. They are derived from embryologic wolffian duct rests.
  Choice B: Incorrect. These are metastatic adenocarcinomas, typically from the stomach.
  Choice C: CORRECT. Also called paratubal cysts, these are common incidental findings. They are embryologic remnants of the wolffian duct. They are not related to neoplasia. This woman had leiomyomas of the uterus which led to the hysterectomy.
  Choice D: Incorrect. PID leads to inflammation with fibrosis, which may result in tubo-ovarian abscess, which is cystic but filled with pus.
  Choice E: Incorrect. These are usually solitary large bulky tumors of the ovary. They are cystic, but often multiloculated, and filled with mucinous secretions.
  Choice F: Incorrect. Fibrocystic disease (or fibrocystic changes) is a diagnosis applied to the breast.
  Choice G: Incorrect. A remnant of the embryologic omphalomesenteric duct may be found anywhere from umbilicus to small bowel; the most common remnant is a Meckel diverticulum. The genital organs are not involved.

QUESTION 4 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q4]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 36-year-old woman has had episodes of lower abdominal and pelvic pain for the past 10 years. A bimanual pelvic examination reveals no abnormalities. A Pap smear is negative. She has an abdominal ultrasound scan that reveals no abnormalities. She undergoes laparoscopy, and several 0.2 to 0.5 cm brown nodular lesions are seen on serosal surfaces of the uterus, fallopian tubes, and appendix. These lesions are excised. Which of the following microscopic findings is most likely to be present in these lesions?

CHOICES:
  A. Endometrial stroma <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  B. Mesothelial cells
  C. Metastatic adenocarcinoma
  D. Capillary proliferation
  E. Granulomatous inflammation

CORRECT ANSWER: A. Endometrial stroma

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Pain is a key feature of endometriosis. The foci of endometriosis are most often found on serosal surfaces in the pelvis, but can be found in other locations such as the surface of the bowel.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: A. Endometrial stroma

CORRECT. Pain is a key feature of endometriosis. The foci of endometriosis are most often found on serosal surfaces in the pelvis, but can be found in other locations such as the surface of the bowel. Endometrial glands, stroma, and hemorrhage are microscopic features.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: CORRECT. Pain is a key feature of endometriosis. The foci of endometriosis are most often found on serosal surfaces in the pelvis, but can be found in other locations such as the surface of the bowel. Endometrial glands, stroma, and hemorrhage are microscopic features.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Endometriosis does not undergo malignant transformation and it is not associated with mesothelial lesions.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Endometriosis (which has glands) is a benign process and not neoplastic.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Though hemangiomas can be found at a variety of locations and can be multiple, they are not typically associated with pain.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Granulomatous inflammation of the pelvis is uncommon; it is unlikely to be hemorrhagic.

QUESTION 5 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q5]
Category: Ovarian Pathology & Neoplasms | Subtopic: Ovarian Pathology & Neoplasms
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 47-year-old woman has noted a pressure sensation, but no pain, in her pelvic region for the past 5 months. On physical examination there is a right adnexal mass. An ultrasound scan shows a 10 cm fluid-filled cystic mass in the right ovary, along with ascitic fluid. A fine needle aspirate of the mass is performed and cytologic examination of clear fluid aspirated from the mass reveals clusters of malignant epithelial cells surrounding psammoma bodies. Which of the following neoplasms is she most likely to have?

CHOICES:
  A. Endometrioid carcinoma
  B. Serous cystadenocarcinoma <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  C. Malignant mesothelioma
  D. Mature cystic teratoma
  E. Squamous cell carcinoma

CORRECT ANSWER: B. Serous cystadenocarcinoma

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Ovarian carcinomas are often associated with ascites, because they have a propensity to seed throughout the peritoneal cavity. Psammoma body formation is common to malignant serous tumors of the ovary.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: B. Serous cystadenocarcinoma

CORRECT. Ovarian carcinomas are often associated with ascites, because they have a propensity to seed throughout the peritoneal cavity. Psammoma body formation is common to malignant serous tumors of the ovary.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. An endometrioid carcinoma of the ovary is usually a solid mass. Cells from such a carcinoma arising in the uterus are usually shed into the endometrial cavity, and psammoma body formation does not occur.
  Choice B: CORRECT. Ovarian carcinomas are often associated with ascites, because they have a propensity to seed throughout the peritoneal cavity. Psammoma body formation is common to malignant serous tumors of the ovary.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Though clusters of mesothelioma cells might be difficult to distinguish from adenocarcinoma, mesotheliomas are quite rare and they do not make psammoma bodies.
  Choice D: Incorrect. A mature cystic teratoma would not have malignant elements.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Squamous cell carcinomas typically arise as a solid mass lesion in the cervical region, as well as vulva and vagina, but they are extremely rare in ovary.
