QUESTION 6 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q6]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 31-year-old woman was last seen by a physician 10 years ago. She had received Pap smear testing in 2 of the 5 years prior. She had no abnormal Pap smears. She now presents with vaginal spotting. On pelvic examination an ulcerated cervical lesion is noted and microscopic examination of cells from this lesion show squamous cell carcinoma. Which of the following conclusions is most appropriate regarding these findings?

CHOICES:
  A. The patient should have continued to return for regular Pap smears <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  B. Herpes simplex viral infection contributed to this problem
  C. The laboratory to which the Pap smears were sent is at fault for missing abnormal cells
  D. Nothing anyone could have done would have prevented this carcinoma
  E. The patient's health insurer is at fault for not covering the full cost of Pap smear testing

CORRECT ANSWER: A. The patient should have continued to return for regular Pap smears

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  It is unlikely that a signficicant lesion will be missed in four consecutive Pap smears, which is the reason for serial Pap smears. The natural history of the progression from dysplasia to invasive carcinoma is usually a decade or more, so there is plenty of time to make a diagnosis and treat conservatively.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: A. The patient should have continued to return for regular Pap smears

CORRECT. It is unlikely that a signficicant lesion will be missed in four consecutive Pap smears, which is the reason for serial Pap smears. The natural history of the progression from dysplasia to invasive carcinoma is usually a decade or more, so there is plenty of time to make a diagnosis and treat conservatively. Cervical carcinoma is a preventable condition. The USPSTF recommends screening for cervical cancer in women age 21 to 65 years with cytology (Pap smear) every 3 years or, for women ages 30 to 65 years who want to lengthen the screening interval, screening with a combination of cytology and HPV testing every 5 years.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: CORRECT. It is unlikely that a signficicant lesion will be missed in four consecutive Pap smears, which is the reason for serial Pap smears. The natural history of the progression from dysplasia to invasive carcinoma is usually a decade or more, so there is plenty of time to make a diagnosis and treat conservatively. Cervical carcinoma is a preventable condition. The USPSTF recommends screening for cervical cancer in women age 21 to 65 years with cytology (Pap smear) every 3 years or, for women ages 30 to 65 years who want to lengthen the screening interval, screening with a combination of cytology and HPV testing every 5 years.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Genital herpes simplex virus infection is not pre-neoplastic.
  Choice C: Incorrect. It is highly unlikely that abnormal cells would be missed in four Pap smears; improper collection technique is more likely.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Invasive cervical carcinomas are potentially preventable through Pap smear screening and simple observation on pelvic examination.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Policies vary markedly, but this is an inexpensive test worth getting regardless of the coverage.

QUESTION 7 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q7]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A quality assurance (QA) project is done by a group of physicians staffing an outpatient clinic to study rates of false negative Pap smears. The QA project reveals that one physician has accounted for half of all the false negative Pap smears collected by the group over the prior year, when compared to follow-up testing. The physician's collection techniques are analyzed, and of the following procedures practiced, which is the only one that is appropriate?

CHOICES:
  A. Wrote the patient's name on the slides <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  B. Air-dried the slides prior to shipping
  C. Advised the patient to have intercourse the day prior to the appointment
  D. Scheduled the patient visit during the menstrual phase of her cycle
  E. Advised the patient to douche the day prior to the appointment
  F. Used a lubricant jelly on the speculum

CORRECT ANSWER: A. Wrote the patient's name on the slides

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  The Pap smear is a very useful test if performed properly. Proper patient identification is key to any laboratory testing procedure.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: A. Wrote the patient's name on the slides

CORRECT. The Pap smear is a very useful test if performed properly. Proper patient identification is key to any laboratory testing procedure.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: CORRECT. The Pap smear is a very useful test if performed properly. Proper patient identification is key to any laboratory testing procedure.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Once the cells are smeared on the slide, they must be fixed quickly to avoid air-drying artefact, which interferes with cytologic interpretation. A collection device that can be directly placed into fixative and sent to the lab is another alternative.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Though not a contraindication, intercourse could introduce substances, such as personal lubricants, which might interfere with the Pap test. Use of vaginal medicines, sprays, or powders should be avoided for at least 24 hours before having a Pap test.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Patients should be instructed to schedule an appointment to collect a Pap smear when they are not in the menstrual phase of the cycle.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Patients should be advised not to douche, as this interferes with the Pap test.
  Choice F: Incorrect. Lubricant jelly can cause artifacts in the Pap smear.


----------------------------------------------------------------------
QUESTION 8 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q8]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 30-year-old infertile woman has had episodic lower abdominal and pelvic pain for 2 years. A physical examination, including pelvic exam, reveals no abnormalities. A Pap smear shows only a few trichomonads and no dysplastic cells. A laparoscopy is performed, and the gynecologist notes the presence of several blue to red 0.2 to 0.4 cm slighted raised lesions scattered on the pelvic peritoneum in the cul-de-sac and broad ligaments. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Metastatic adenocarcinoma
  B. <em>Neisseria gonorrheae</em> infection
  C. Endometriosis <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  D. Candidiasis
  E. Leiomyomata

CORRECT ANSWER: C. Endometriosis

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  These 'powder burns' are typical for the small hemorrhagic foci of endometriosis on pelvic peritoneum that can produce so much discomfort for their size.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: C. Endometriosis

CORRECT. These 'powder burns' are typical for the small hemorrhagic foci of endometriosis on pelvic peritoneum that can produce so much discomfort for their size.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Metastases should produce mass lesions, and she would become severly debilitated after several years with an extensive carcinoma.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Gonorrheal infections can produce salpingitis that can be severe, but it usually resolves in several weeks, though pelvic inflammatory disease can continue to produce pain. There is scarring, but no focal red or blue lesions.
  Choice C: CORRECT. These 'powder burns' are typical for the small hemorrhagic foci of endometriosis on pelvic peritoneum that can produce so much discomfort for their size.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Yeast infections involve the vagina, producing discharge and local irritation.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Leiomyomata can be large enough to cause pelvic discomfort, but they are firm masses.

QUESTION 9 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q9]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 43-year-old woman has noted menstrual periods that have been exceptionally heavy, lasting 6 to 8 days, for 4 months. She has also noted minor intermenstrual bleeding. On physical examination her uterine cervix appears normal, and a Pap smear shows no abnormal cells. Pelvic examination reveals that the uterus is enlarged to twice normal size and is nodular. There are no adnexal masses. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Endometrial carcinoma
  B. Leiomyomata <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  C. Ectopic pregnancy
  D. Endometriosis
  E. Secondary syphilis

CORRECT ANSWER: B. Leiomyomata

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  The most common neoplasm of the uterus is a leiomyoma. They can be multiple, can slowly enlarge, and can lead to abnormal bleeding.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: B. Leiomyomata

CORRECT. The most common neoplasm of the uterus is a leiomyoma. They can be multiple, can slowly enlarge, and can lead to abnormal bleeding.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. An endometrial carcinoma does not greatly enlarge the uterus. They are most common in postmenopausal women.
  Choice B: CORRECT. The most common neoplasm of the uterus is a leiomyoma. They can be multiple, can slowly enlarge, and can lead to abnormal bleeding.
  Choice C: Incorrect. An ectopic pregnancy is most likely to occur in the fallopian tube, leading to a small mass that ruptures and bleeds, producing a medical emergency. The blood may extend into the peritoneal cavity, where it can be detected by culdocentesis.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Endometriosis consists of small foci no more than a few millimeters in size. Sometimes a focus of endometriosis can give rise to an endometrioma, a mass lesion filled with blood, but these are typically external to the uterus, most often involving ovary.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Syphilis can produce a 'hard' painless chancre on external genitalia or cervix, but no masses.

QUESTION 10 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q10]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 43-year-old woman has noted increasing abdominal discomfort for the past year. On physical examination, there is no tenderness. Bowel sounds are present. She has no vaginal bleeding. An abdominal ultrasound reveals a 7 cm left adnexal mass. A total abdominal hysterectomy is performed. Pathologic examination of the ovarian mass reveals it is unilocular, filled with watery fluid, and lined with papillary excrescences. On microscopic examination there is no infiltration of the underlying stroma. Which of the following neoplasms is this woman most likely to have?

CHOICES:
  A. Granulosa-theca cell tumors
  B. Mature cystic teratomas
  C. Fibrosarcoma with metastases
  D. Borderline serous tumor <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  E. Clear cell carcinomas

CORRECT ANSWER: D. Borderline serous tumor

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  This is a classic appearance for ovarian serous tumor, and most of them are benign or borderline, particularly if presenting in reproductive years. However, the gross appearance alone is not diagnostic, and a borderline serous tumor must be distinguished microscopically.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: D. Borderline serous tumor

CORRECT. This is a classic appearance for ovarian serous tumor, and most of them are benign or borderline, particularly if presenting in reproductive years. However, the gross appearance alone is not diagnostic, and a borderline serous tumor must be distinguished microscopically. Invasiveness would suggest malignancy.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Granulosa-theca cell tumors or fibrothecomas are solid tumors.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Such 'dermoid cysts' are often filled with sebaceous material and lots of hair.
  Choice C: Incorrect. This is rare. Sarcomas are typically large, solid masses.
  Choice D: CORRECT. This is a classic appearance for ovarian serous tumor, and most of them are benign or borderline, particularly if presenting in reproductive years. However, the gross appearance alone is not diagnostic, and a borderline serous tumor must be distinguished microscopically. Invasiveness would suggest malignancy.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Clear cell carcinomas of the ovary are usually solid tumors; they are less common than cystadenocarcinomas.

QUESTION 11 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q11]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 28-year-old sexually active woman undergoes a routine examination. Pelvic examination reveals no abnormalities. A Pap smear is obtained. The cytopathology report indicates the presence of severely dysplastic cells (high grade squamous intraepithelial lesion, or HSIL). A biopsy of the cervix is performed, and on microscopic examination shows cervical intraepithelial neoplasia III (CIN III). Infection with which of the following organisms is most likely to cause her disease?

CHOICES:
  A. Herpes simplex virus infection
  B. Epstein-Barr virus
  C. <em>Candida albicans</em>
  D. Human papillomavirus <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  E. <em>Trichomonas vaginalis</em>
  F. <em>Gardnerella vaginalis</em>

CORRECT ANSWER: D. Human papillomavirus

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  HPV infections can lead to squamous epithelial dysplasia and carcinoma, particularly infection with high grade subtypes such as HPV 16 and 18. The HPV vaccine immunizes agains these subtypes, as well as the most common subtypes 6 and 11 associated with condyloma.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: D. Human papillomavirus

CORRECT. HPV infections can lead to squamous epithelial dysplasia and carcinoma, particularly infection with high grade subtypes such as HPV 16 and 18. The HPV vaccine immunizes agains these subtypes, as well as the most common subtypes 6 and 11 associated with condyloma.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. HSV produces an infection with recurrent crops of painful vesicles, but these lesions are no pre-neoplastic.
  Choice B: Incorrect. EBV infection is not characteristic for the genital tract.
  Choice C: Incorrect. <em>Candida</em>l infections can produce vaginitis, but are not premalignant.
  Choice D: CORRECT. HPV infections can lead to squamous epithelial dysplasia and carcinoma, particularly infection with high grade subtypes such as HPV 16 and 18. The HPV vaccine immunizes agains these subtypes, as well as the most common subtypes 6 and 11 associated with condyloma.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Trichomonal infections are typically mild and do not presage CIN.
  Choice F: Incorrect. <em>G. vaginalis</em> is a cause for bacterial vaginosis, not dysplasia.

QUESTION 12 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q12]
Category: Endometrial & Uterine Pathology | Subtopic: Endometrial & Uterine Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 23-year-old woman has a 1 day history of increasing obtundation. On physical examination her temperature is 37.3°C, pulse 96/minute, respirations 18/minute, and blood pressure 90/45 mm Hg. A culdocentesis yields no blood. Abdominal ultrasound shows an intrauterine gestational sac and 12 week fetus. Laboratory studies show a positive pregnancy test. She has a WBC count of 11,400/microliter, Hgb 10.6 g/dL, Hct 31.1%, MCV 95 fL, and platelet count 26,400/microliter. Schistocytes are noted on her peripheral blood smear. Her protime is 44 sec, partial thromboplastin time 61 sec, and D-Dimer 16 microgm/mL. A urinalysis dipstick examination shows no blood, protein, or glucose. Which of the following complications of pregnancy is most likely to explain her condition?

CHOICES:
  A. Placental infarction
  B. Eclampsia
  C. Amniotic fluid embolus
  D. Retained dead fetus <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  E. Ruptured tubal ectopic

CORRECT ANSWER: D. Retained dead fetus

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  This complication results in release of thromboplastin into the circulation, promoting disseminated intravascular coagulation (DIC) ith consumption of clotting factors and thrombocytopenia. In this case, the ultrasound showed an intrauterine pregnancy was present, though detection of fetal movement would be difficult at 12 weeks, but a heartbeat could be detected as a sign of life.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: D. Retained dead fetus

CORRECT. This complication results in release of thromboplastin into the circulation, promoting disseminated intravascular coagulation (DIC) ith consumption of clotting factors and thrombocytopenia. In this case, the ultrasound showed an intrauterine pregnancy was present, though detection of fetal movement would be difficult at 12 weeks, but a heartbeat could be detected as a sign of life.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Most placental infarcts are small and insignificant and do not lead to disseminated intravascular coagulation (DIC).
  Choice B: Incorrect. Eclampsia can lead to uteroplacental insufficiency with ischemia, leading to endothelial injury that promotes disseminated intravascular coagulation (DIC). However, there should be hypertension, proteinuria, and seizures.
  Choice C: Incorrect. This is a rare complication of late third trimester pregnancy with a high mortality rate. Thromboplastins released into the circulation promotes disseminated intravascular coagulation (DIC). However, patients typically present with dypsnea.
  Choice D: CORRECT. This complication results in release of thromboplastin into the circulation, promoting disseminated intravascular coagulation (DIC) ith consumption of clotting factors and thrombocytopenia. In this case, the ultrasound showed an intrauterine pregnancy was present, though detection of fetal movement would be difficult at 12 weeks, but a heartbeat could be detected as a sign of life.
  Choice E: Incorrect. The shock resulting from the rupture leads to hypoxia and endothelial damage with resultant disseminated intravascular coagulation (DIC). However, in this case the culdocentesis was negative and the pregnancy was intrauterine.

QUESTION 13 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q13]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 22-year-old woman passes grape-like masses of tissue per vagina in the 16th week of her first pregnancy. She had not felt any fetal movement at any time. On physical examination she measures 18 weeks in size. A D&C is performed, yielding 1000 cc of 0.5 to 1.5 cm fluid-filled vesicles. Microscopic examination of this tissue shows large avascular villi along with trophoblastic proliferation. Which of the following is the best method to employ for her follow-up care?

CHOICES:
  A. Chest radiograph
  B. Serum beta-HCG <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  C. Endometrial biopsy
  D. Pelvic ultrasound
  E. Pap smear

CORRECT ANSWER: B. Serum beta-HCG

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  She has a hydatidiform mole. Persistence or an increase in the HCG level should suggest that trophoblastic disease is still present, such as invasive mole or choriocarcinoma.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: B. Serum beta-HCG

CORRECT. She has a hydatidiform mole. Persistence or an increase in the HCG level should suggest that trophoblastic disease is still present, such as invasive mole or choriocarcinoma.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. The appearance of lung metastases from a choriocarcinoma arising in this hydatidiform mole would be a late finding, and after persistence of an elevated HCG.
  Choice B: CORRECT. She has a hydatidiform mole. Persistence or an increase in the HCG level should suggest that trophoblastic disease is still present, such as invasive mole or choriocarcinoma.
  Choice C: Incorrect. The biopsy might not pick up invasive mole or metastatic choriocarcinoma.
  Choice D: Incorrect. The ultrasound could demonstrate mass lesions, but these would not be early findings.
  Choice E: Incorrect. A Pap smear is relatively insensitive for endometrial lesions, and it certainly would not indicate invasive mole or metastasis of a choriocarcinoma.

QUESTION 14 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q14]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 23-year-old woman has the sudden onset of lower abdominal pain. Pelvic examination reveals a normal sized uterus and normal appearing cervix and vagina. However, there is marked tenderness upon palpation of the left adnexal region. A transvaginal ultrasound shows no intrauterine gestational sac, but there is a 2 cm left adnexal mass. Culdoscocentesis yields bloody fluid. Which of the following is most useful procedure to aid in diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Serum complement determination
  B. Microscopic urinalysis
  C. Pap smear
  D. Serum beta-HCG <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  E. Endometrial biopsy
  F. WBC count

CORRECT ANSWER: D. Serum beta-HCG

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  You should strongly suspect a ruptured ectopic pregnancy in this setting. The pregnancy test would likely have been performed first.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: D. Serum beta-HCG

CORRECT. You should strongly suspect a ruptured ectopic pregnancy in this setting. The pregnancy test would likely have been performed first. Culdocentesis with blood suggests that the ectopic has ruptured, which is a medical emergency.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Complement levels are used to solve complex problems with immunologic and autoimmune diseases, which are chronic in nature.
  Choice B: Incorrect. A routine urinalysis does not include a pregnancy test or a determination of HCG level.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Besides, this is not a 'STAT' test. The Pap smear is useful for screening for dysplasia or carcinoma and for some infections.
  Choice D: CORRECT. You should strongly suspect a ruptured ectopic pregnancy in this setting. The pregnancy test would likely have been performed first. Culdocentesis with blood suggests that the ectopic has ruptured, which is a medical emergency.
  Choice E: Incorrect. The endometrial biopsy might be a next step with a positive HCG to help determine if the pregnancy is ectopic.
  Choice F: Incorrect. She does not have a reported fever. Although an inflammatory process is possible, there is a diagnosis higher on the list here.

QUESTION 15 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q15]
Category: Endometrial & Uterine Pathology | Subtopic: Endometrial & Uterine Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 20-year-old woman has had irregular menstrual bleeding for the past 6 months. On physical examination there are no abnormal findings. Laboratory studies show a negative pregnancy test. A D&C is performed and microscopic examination shows dyssynchronous endometrial glands and stroma of anovulatory cycles. Following the procedure she ceases to have menstrual bleeding. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Endometriosis
  B. Asherman syndrome <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  C. Prolactinoma
  D. Ovarian failure
  E. Oral contraceptive use

CORRECT ANSWER: B. Asherman syndrome

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  This is a very rare complication of a D&C procedure in which endometrium, including the stratum basalis, is removed, preventing normal growth and cycling of the endometrium.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: B. Asherman syndrome

CORRECT. This is a very rare complication of a D&C procedure in which endometrium, including the stratum basalis, is removed, preventing normal growth and cycling of the endometrium.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Endometriosis is best known as a cause for pelvic pain, though it may cause irregular menstrual cycles. It is unlikely to cause amenorrhea.
  Choice B: CORRECT. This is a very rare complication of a D&C procedure in which endometrium, including the stratum basalis, is removed, preventing normal growth and cycling of the endometrium.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Elevated prolactin levels can produce amenorrhea along with galactorrhea. The onset is not related to a D&C procedure.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Ovarian failure is unlikely to have a relationship to a D&C.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Oral contraceptives produce more regular cycles.

QUESTION 16 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q16]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 14-year-old girl has had pelvic pain for the past 3 months. She has not yet noted passing menstrual blood. She has not had any sexual activity. She refuses physical examination, but an abdominal ultrasound shows fluid distending the vagina. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Cervical condyloma
  B. Endometriosis
  C. Cervical gonorrhea
  D. Imperforate hymen <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  E. Ruptured Bartholin cyst

CORRECT ANSWER: D. Imperforate hymen

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Following the onset of menarche, the blockage to menstrual flow from the imperforate hymen leads to an accumulation of blood in the vagina, called hematocolpos.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: D. Imperforate hymen

CORRECT. Following the onset of menarche, the blockage to menstrual flow from the imperforate hymen leads to an accumulation of blood in the vagina, called hematocolpos.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Condylomas consist of thickened epithelium that does not bleed.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Endometriotic lesions are usually located on serosal surfaces in the pelvis and abdomen. There is minimal hemorrhage just at the site of the lesion itself. The onset of pelvic pain is typically at a later age than that of this patient.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Gonorrhea can produce inflammation, but not hemorrhage. Gonorrhea is a sexually transmitted disease.
  Choice D: CORRECT. Following the onset of menarche, the blockage to menstrual flow from the imperforate hymen leads to an accumulation of blood in the vagina, called hematocolpos.
  Choice E: Incorrect. A Bartholin cyst will produced a localized mass that will contain clear fluid, and when inflamed may contain purulent material.

QUESTION 17 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q17]

----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 64-year-old woman has had itching with irritation of the vulvar region, along with vaginal dryness, for the past 8 months. On physical examination she has pale grey patches from 1 to 2 cm in size on the vulva. Biopsy of one lesion is performed and on microscopic examination shows epithelial thinning, dermal fibrosis, and perivascular chronic inflammation. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Squamous cell carcinoma
  B. Lichen sclerosus <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  C. Condyloma acuminatum
  D. Adenosis
  E. Extramammary Paget disease
  F. Dermatophyte infection

CORRECT ANSWER: B. Lichen sclerosus

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Lichen sclerosus et atrophicus is typically seen after menopause and is slowly progressive. It may predispose to infection.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: B. Lichen sclerosus

CORRECT. Lichen sclerosus et atrophicus is typically seen after menopause and is slowly progressive. It may predispose to infection. Lichen sclerosis itself is not premalignant, but persons with it have a slightly increased risk for cancer.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. This is a neoplasm that would lead to a mass with malignant squamous epithelial cells.
  Choice B: CORRECT. Lichen sclerosus et atrophicus is typically seen after menopause and is slowly progressive. It may predispose to infection. Lichen sclerosis itself is not premalignant, but persons with it have a slightly increased risk for cancer.
  Choice C: Incorrect. This is a 'venereal wart' from human papillomavirus (HPV) infection, leading to verrucous pink to white plaques that histologically have epithelial hyperplasia and koilocytosis.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Vaginal adenosis may occur in women with exposure to diethylstilbestrol in utero.
  Choice E: Incorrect. This is a red, crusted area that has clusters of malignant epithelial cells within the epidermis and its appendages.
  Choice F: Incorrect. This would be the female equivalent of 'jock itch' in men. Jock itch (tinea cruris) is a dermatophyte fungal infection associated with a red and itchy rash in warm and moist areas of the body, including the groin and inner thighs. There would likely be hyperkeratosis and minimal inflammation.

QUESTION 18 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q18]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
For the past year, a 50-year-old woman has noted that her menstrual periods have been exceptionally heavy and last 7 to 9 days. She has noted occasional minor intermenstrual bleeding. For the past 3 months, she has been taking supplemental dietary iron for iron deficiency anemia. On pelvic examination, the uterine cervix appears normal, but the uterus is enlarged to twice normal size. Transvaginal ultrasound reveals the presence of a 9 cm solid mass in the uterus. A hysterectomy is performed, and on gross inspection with sectioning the uterus a reddish-tan mass is found with a fleshy cut surface. Microscopically the mass is highly cellular, with spindle cells having hyperchromatic nuclei and 10 to 20 mitoses per high power field. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Endometrial polyp
  B. Adenomyosis
  C. Embryonal rhabdomyosarcoma
  D. Atypical hyperplasia
  E. Leiomyosarcoma <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  F. Carcinosarcoma

CORRECT ANSWER: E. Leiomyosarcoma

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Leiomyosarcomas of the uterus are uncommon malignant neoplasms. They do not arise from leiomyomas.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: E. Leiomyosarcoma

CORRECT. Leiomyosarcomas of the uterus are uncommon malignant neoplasms. They do not arise from leiomyomas. They are larger and more cellular than benign leiomyomas, and leiomyosarcomas have mitotic figures. At least 20% of women have at least one leiomyoma, but leiomyosarcomas are rare. However, a large cellular mass with mitoses is less likely to be a leiomyoma. Postmenopausally, most leiomyomas stop growing or even regress

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. The endometrial polyp is composed of both glands and stroma.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Adenomyosis, which is the presence of endometrial glands in the uterine myometrium, results in diffuse uterine enlargement, and is not very common.
  Choice C: Incorrect. An embryonal rhabdomyosarcoma is also called sarcoma botryoides, and like primitive neoplasms elsewhere that resemble embryonal structures, they are most likely to arise in children, and most often the vaginal region in girls.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Atypical hyperplasia may precede endometrial carcinoma. The hyperplasia is not a mass lesion and is unlikely to increase the size of the uterus.
  Choice E: CORRECT. Leiomyosarcomas of the uterus are uncommon malignant neoplasms. They do not arise from leiomyomas. They are larger and more cellular than benign leiomyomas, and leiomyosarcomas have mitotic figures. At least 20% of women have at least one leiomyoma, but leiomyosarcomas are rare. However, a large cellular mass with mitoses is less likely to be a leiomyoma. Postmenopausally, most leiomyomas stop growing or even regress
  Choice F: Incorrect. A carcinosarcoma has malignant components that are both epithelial and stromal. The stromal component does not always resemble just smooth muscle but may have sarcomatous elements resembling other mesodermal tissues; these heterologous elements can resemble malignant cartilage or bone. The 3MT also tends to be a large, bulky mass.

QUESTION 19 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q19]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 31-year-old G3 P2002 woman is at 14 weeks gestation, but has noted a small amount of vaginal bleeding for the past 2 weeks. Laboratory studies show an HCG level of 650,000 U/L. An ultrasound shows a 'snowstorm' for intrauterine contents but no identifiable fetus. A D&C is performed with evacuation of 500 mL of grape-like vesicles. A month later her vaginal bleeding persists and her serum beta-HCG is 35,000 U/L. Which of the following pathologic abnormalities is most likely to be present in this woman?

CHOICES:
  A. Pulmonary metastases
  B. Tubal ectopic pregnancy
  C. Endometritis
  D. Placental site trophoblastic tumor
  E. Invasive mole <-- CORRECT ANSWER

CORRECT ANSWER: E. Invasive mole

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Invasive moles constitute about 10% of gestational trophoblastic disease. The failure of the HCG to diminish to near negative following the D&C suggests residual gestational trophoblastic disease, either invasive mole or less frequently choriocarcinoma.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: E. Invasive mole

CORRECT. Invasive moles constitute about 10% of gestational trophoblastic disease. The failure of the HCG to diminish to near negative following the D&C suggests residual gestational trophoblastic disease, either invasive mole or less frequently choriocarcinoma.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Pulmonary masses would be typical for metastatic choriocarcinoma, but choriocarcinoma complicates only about 2 to 3 % of molar pregnancies.
  Choice B: Incorrect. If the pregnancy were tubal, it would not typically last more than a month before rupture of the tube occurred, and the HCG level, not high to begin with, would fall soon thereafter.
  Choice C: Incorrect. An endometritis would not account for a continued high HCG level.
  Choice D: Incorrect. This is a rare tumor that consists of intermediate trophoblast that invades deeply into the myometrium. The cells make human placental lactogen, not HCG. There is no cytotrophoblast or syncytiotrophoblast. These tumors are locally invasive but often self-limited.
  Choice E: CORRECT. Invasive moles constitute about 10% of gestational trophoblastic disease. The failure of the HCG to diminish to near negative following the D&C suggests residual gestational trophoblastic disease, either invasive mole or less frequently choriocarcinoma.

QUESTION 20 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q20]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 33-year-old woman and her husband have wanted a child, but she has been unable to conceive for the past 10 years. She reports mild pelvic pain, but regular menstrual cycles. On physical examination the cervix and vagina appear normal. The uterus is normal on palpation, but adnexa are mildly tender. A pelvic ultrasound shows slightly thickened fallopian tubes. A Pap smear is normal. Her husband's sperm count is normal. Which of the following is most likely to be the cause for her infertility?

CHOICES:
  A. Adenomyosis
  B. Adenohypophyseal prolactinoma
  C. Pelvic inflammatory disease <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  D. Mature cystic teratoma
  E. Chronic cervicitis

CORRECT ANSWER: C. Pelvic inflammatory disease

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  PID leads to scarring that interferes with movement of the ovum released from the ovary down the fallopian tube. PID can lead to infertility.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: C. Pelvic inflammatory disease

CORRECT. PID leads to scarring that interferes with movement of the ovum released from the ovary down the fallopian tube. PID can lead to infertility. Assisted reproductive technology would be needed in her case.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Adenomyosis can produce irregular menstrual cycles and irregular vaginal bleeding. The uterus is typically enlarged.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Amenorrhea and dysmenorrhea are the classic findings from the secretion of prolactin by a pituitary adenoma. There is often galactorrhea as well.
  Choice C: CORRECT. PID leads to scarring that interferes with movement of the ovum released from the ovary down the fallopian tube. PID can lead to infertility. Assisted reproductive technology would be needed in her case.
  Choice D: Incorrect. A teratoma is most often unilateral, though occasionally bilateral. Teratomas are mass lesions that can often be felt, and should be evident with ultrasound.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Many women have some degree of chronic cervicitis, and this has no significant impact upon fertility.

QUESTION 22 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q22]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 19-year-old woman has noted increasing size and number of warty lesions on her external genitalia for the past 5 years. On physical examination she has several pink-tan rounded 1 to 2 cm slightly raised, rough lesions on the perineum and vulva. Biopsy of one lesion is performed and on microscopic examination shows acanthosis of squamous epithelium along with koilocytosis. Which of the following is the most likely risk factor for these lesions?

CHOICES:
  A. Irritant chemical exposure
  B. Oral contraceptive use
  C. Candida vulvovaginitis
  D. Multiple sexual partners <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  E. Turner syndrome

CORRECT ANSWER: D. Multiple sexual partners

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Condyloma accuminatum is one of the manifestations of human papillomavirus infection (HPV). The most common subtypes associated with condyloma are 6 and 11.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: D. Multiple sexual partners

CORRECT. Condyloma accuminatum is one of the manifestations of human papillomavirus infection (HPV). The most common subtypes associated with condyloma are 6 and 11. Though condyloma is not premalignant, the risk factor is the same, and she could acquire subtypes of HPV, such as 16 and 18, that do increase the risk for squamous dysplasias and carcinomas of the cervix. HPV vaccines will protect against subtypes 6, 11, 16, and 18.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Condylomas are not the result of irritant inflammation, which would lead to an eczematous dermatitis with redness and scaling.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Oral contraceptives are unlikely to have an effect upon external female genitalia.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Condylomata have an association with human papillomavirus (HPV) infection, but not candidiasis.
  Choice D: CORRECT. Condyloma accuminatum is one of the manifestations of human papillomavirus infection (HPV). The most common subtypes associated with condyloma are 6 and 11. Though condyloma is not premalignant, the risk factor is the same, and she could acquire subtypes of HPV, such as 16 and 18, that do increase the risk for squamous dysplasias and carcinomas of the cervix. HPV vaccines will protect against subtypes 6, 11, 16, and 18.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Turner syndrome (monosomy X) leads to reduced or absent ovarian function and infertility. There is no risk for development of squamous proliferations.

QUESTION 23 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q23]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 40-year-old woman has experienced pelvic discomfort for over a month, along with a 4 kg weight loss, nervousness, and diaphoresis. A pelvic examination reveals a large left adnexal mass that, on transvaginal ultrasound, appears as a discrete 10 cm cystic and solid mass. The uterus appears normal in size. A Pap smear is normal. The mass is removed and on gross pathologic examination is filled with hair and sebum, along with solid tan areas next to the smooth-surfaced outer wall. Which of the following laboratory test findings is most likely to have been present just prior to her surgery?

CHOICES:
  A. Thyroxine of 11.3 microgm/dL <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  B. HCG of 45,000 IU/L
  C. Potassium of 2.9 mmol/L
  D. Estradiol of 1700 pg/mL
  E. Cancer antigen 125 of 540 U/mL

CORRECT ANSWER: A. Thyroxine of 11.3 microgm/dL

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  The abundant hair is typical for the ectodermal component of a mature cystic teratoma, but mesodermal and endodermal components will also be present. The historical findings here are consistent with hyperthyroidism and suggest that this teratoma also has thyroid tissue, known as struma ovarii when the majority of the teratoma is composed of thyroid tissue.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: A. Thyroxine of 11.3 microgm/dL

CORRECT. The abundant hair is typical for the ectodermal component of a mature cystic teratoma, but mesodermal and endodermal components will also be present. The historical findings here are consistent with hyperthyroidism and suggest that this teratoma also has thyroid tissue, known as struma ovarii when the majority of the teratoma is composed of thyroid tissue. It is a rare cause for hyperthyroidism.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: CORRECT. The abundant hair is typical for the ectodermal component of a mature cystic teratoma, but mesodermal and endodermal components will also be present. The historical findings here are consistent with hyperthyroidism and suggest that this teratoma also has thyroid tissue, known as struma ovarii when the majority of the teratoma is composed of thyroid tissue. It is a rare cause for hyperthyroidism.
  Choice B: Incorrect. An elevated HCG could indicate an intrauterine pregnancy or a gestational trophoblastic disease. Some choriocarcinomas are associated with hyperthyroidism. Grossly, choriocarcinomas are not discrete masses, but bloody messes.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Hypokalemia is seen with aldosteronomas of the adrenal gland.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Elevated estrogen levels are seen with pregnancy, but high levels such as this are rarely present, even with neoplasms such as fibrothecomas.
  Choice E: Incorrect. The CA-125 is a marker for ovarian malignancies.

QUESTION 24 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q24]
Category: Endometrial & Uterine Pathology | Subtopic: Endometrial & Uterine Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 32-year-old G4 P3003 woman goes for a routine prenatal check at 12 weeks gestation. On physical examination she is found to be large for dates, and no fetal heart tones are audible. An ultrasound is performed and revealed that no fetus was present, only many echogenic cystic areas within the uterus. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Invasive mole
  B. Partial hydatidiform mole
  C. Placental site trophoblastic tumor
  D. Choriocarcinoma
  E. Complete hydatidiform mole <-- CORRECT ANSWER

CORRECT ANSWER: E. Complete hydatidiform mole

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  No fetus is present in this case, only grape-like chorionic villi that produce the echogenic effect on ultrasound. Complete mole is the most common form of gestational trophoblastic disease.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: E. Complete hydatidiform mole

CORRECT. No fetus is present in this case, only grape-like chorionic villi that produce the echogenic effect on ultrasound. Complete mole is the most common form of gestational trophoblastic disease.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. An invasive mole is less common than a complete mole.
  Choice B: Incorrect. A fetus is present with a partial mole, which is much less common than a complete mole.
  Choice C: Incorrect. This is a rare tumor that consists of intermediate trophoblast that invades deeply into the myometrium. The cells make human placental lactogen, not HCG. There is no cytotrophoblast or syncytiotrophoblast. These tumors are locally invasive but often self-limited to the pregnanc.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Choriocarcinomas are solid, but hemorrhagic lesions that invade the myometrial wall, though the uterus can be enlarged. They can follow a hydatidiform mole, but are much less common.
  Choice E: CORRECT. No fetus is present in this case, only grape-like chorionic villi that produce the echogenic effect on ultrasound. Complete mole is the most common form of gestational trophoblastic disease.

QUESTION 25 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q25]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 37-year-old woman goes to her physician for a routine checkup. On physical examination there are no abnormal findings. A Pap smear is taken and cytologically there are dysplastic cells present. A cervical biopsy is performed. Microscopic examination shows dysplasia involving the full thickness of the cervical epithelium. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. High grade squamous intraepithelial lesion (HSIL) <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  B. Severe chronic cervicitis with herpes simplex virus (HSV)
  C. Diethylstilbestrol (DES) exposure
  D. Endocervical adenocarcinoma
  E. Extramammary Paget disease

CORRECT ANSWER: A. High grade squamous intraepithelial lesion (HSIL)

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  An HSIL is equivalent to cervical intraepithelial neoplasia (CIN) III, or severe dysplasia. With proper therapy, the lesion will be removed and not be given a chance to progress to invasive carcinoma.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: A. High grade squamous intraepithelial lesion (HSIL)

CORRECT. An HSIL is equivalent to cervical intraepithelial neoplasia (CIN) III, or severe dysplasia. With proper therapy, the lesion will be removed and not be given a chance to progress to invasive carcinoma.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: CORRECT. An HSIL is equivalent to cervical intraepithelial neoplasia (CIN) III, or severe dysplasia. With proper therapy, the lesion will be removed and not be given a chance to progress to invasive carcinoma.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Inflammation will produce some inflammatory atypia, but not dysplasia.
  Choice C: Incorrect. DES exposure in utero predisposes women to vaginal clear cell carcinoma and to adenosis.
  Choice D: Incorrect. DES exposure can lead to microglandular hyperplasia and to vaginal clear cell carcinomas.
  Choice E: Incorrect. This is seen on the vulva and consists of malignant cells in the epithelium that are mucin positive, indicating adenocarcinoma.

QUESTION 26 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q26]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
Two weeks after delivery of a normal healthy term infant boy following an uncomplicated pregnancy, a 25-year-old woman still has a vaginal discharge consisting of dark brown, foul-smelling material. Her temperature is 37.4°C. On pelvic examination, the uterus is slightly enlarged and she has pelvic tenderness. Laboratory studies show her serum beta-HCG is negative. Which of the following conditions is she most likely to have?

CHOICES:
  A. Invasive mole
  B. Degenerating uterine leiomyoma
  C. Postpartum pituitary necrosis
  D. Endometriosis
  E. Retained products of conception <-- CORRECT ANSWER

CORRECT ANSWER: E. Retained products of conception

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  The retained placental fragements are a source for infection producing an acute endometritis. A D&C must be done to remove the fragments.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: E. Retained products of conception

CORRECT. The retained placental fragements are a source for infection producing an acute endometritis. A D&C must be done to remove the fragments. At the time of delivery, the placenta should be checked for completeness; a missing cotyledon suggests incomplete placental delivery.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Her beta-HCG is negative, so molar pregnancy is ruled out.
  Choice B: Incorrect. A leiomyoma may degenerate and abort following delivery, but this is a rare event.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Anterior pituitary necrosis leads to lack of lactation and other evidence for hypopituitarism which are signs of Sheehan syndrome. This typically occurs when there is marked hypotension from blood loss at the time of delivery.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Endometriosis is the presence of endometrial glands and stroma outside the endometrium, producing pain but no discharge.
  Choice E: CORRECT. The retained placental fragements are a source for infection producing an acute endometritis. A D&C must be done to remove the fragments. At the time of delivery, the placenta should be checked for completeness; a missing cotyledon suggests incomplete placental delivery.

QUESTION 27 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q27]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 39-year-old woman has had dysmenorrhea for 7 months. On pelvic examination, there are no adnexal masses, and the cervix appears normal, but the uterus is three times normal size. The uterus appears to be symmetrically enlarged on abdominal ultrasound, with no masses present and an endometrial cavity that is nearly normal in size. A Pap smear is normal. Her serum pregnancy test is negative. A total abdominal hysterectomy is performed. Which of the following microscopic findings is most likely to be present in her uterus?

CHOICES:
  A. Atypical glands invading through the uterine wall
  B. Large avascular villi with trophoblastic proliferation
  C. Hyperchromatic and pleomorphic smooth muscle cells
  D. Extensive acute inflammation
  E. Endometrial glands and stroma in the myometrium <-- CORRECT ANSWER

CORRECT ANSWER: E. Endometrial glands and stroma in the myometrium

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Adenomyosis can produce diffuse uterine enlargement.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: E. Endometrial glands and stroma in the myometrium

CORRECT. Adenomyosis can produce diffuse uterine enlargement.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. An endometrial adenocarcinoma is most likely to occur at a postmenopausal age, and it is not likely to greatly enlarge the uterus. If large, the mass is likely to be irregular, not symmetric.
  Choice B: Incorrect. The avascular villi with trophoblastic proliferation are features of a molar pregnancy.
  Choice C: Incorrect. A leiomyosarcoma is usually a large, solitary mass.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Endometritis does not typically enlarge the uterus.
  Choice E: CORRECT. Adenomyosis can produce diffuse uterine enlargement.

QUESTION 28 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q28]
Category: Endometrial & Uterine Pathology | Subtopic: Endometrial & Uterine Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 15-year-old girl has had irregular menstrual cycles for 5 months. She had menarche at age 13. There are no abnormal physical examination findings. An endometrial biopsy is taken on post-ovulatory day 9 and shows proliferative phase endometrium. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Adenomyosis
  B. Anovulatory cycles <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  C. Endometrial adenocarcinoma
  D. Leiomyomata
  E. Oral contraceptive use
  F. Ovarian thecoma

CORRECT ANSWER: B. Anovulatory cycles

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Anovulatory cycles occur most often at the time of menarche, menopause, or with ovarian dysfunction such as in polycystic ovarian disease. The endometrium is not cycling normally.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: B. Anovulatory cycles

CORRECT. Anovulatory cycles occur most often at the time of menarche, menopause, or with ovarian dysfunction such as in polycystic ovarian disease. The endometrium is not cycling normally.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Adenomyosis is uncommon at this age. It could produce irregular bleeding. The uterus is usually enlarged.
  Choice B: CORRECT. Anovulatory cycles occur most often at the time of menarche, menopause, or with ovarian dysfunction such as in polycystic ovarian disease. The endometrium is not cycling normally.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Endometrial adenocarcinoma is rare at this age.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Leiomyomas may produce irregular bleeding. It would be rare to have leiomyomas of sufficient size to produce problems at her age.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Oral contraceptive use produces regular menstrual cycles.
  Choice F: Incorrect. Ovarian thecomas could produce estrogens and cause endometrial hyperplasia and bleeding. An ovarian tumor is rare at her age, and it would produce a mass lesion.

QUESTION 29 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q29]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 29-year-old woman has had dyspareunia for the past 2 months. She has had only one sexual partner--her husband. She is G2 P2002. On physical examination there are no abnormal findings except for a small but slightly tender 1.5 cm mass located in the right lateral wall of her vagina. The lesion is excised. On gross examination this mass is cystic and fluid filled. On microscopic examination the cyst is lined by a cuboidal epithelium. Which of the following is the most likely etiology for this lesion?

CHOICES:
  A. Bacterial vaginosis
  B. Embryologic remnant <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  C. Gonoccocal infection
  D. Metastatic adenocarcinoma
  E. Oral contraceptive use
  F. Foreign body

CORRECT ANSWER: B. Embryologic remnant

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  This is a Gartner duct cyst, which is a remnant of the mesonephric duct in the lateral vaginal wall. Such lesions can be multiple.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: B. Embryologic remnant

CORRECT. This is a Gartner duct cyst, which is a remnant of the mesonephric duct in the lateral vaginal wall. Such lesions can be multiple. There is no risk for malignancy. A Bartholin gland (greater vestibular gland) cyst may arise in the vagina, just inside the entrance in the posterior vestibule.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Bacterial vaginosis produces a vaginal discharge and discomfort, but no mass lesion.
  Choice B: CORRECT. This is a Gartner duct cyst, which is a remnant of the mesonephric duct in the lateral vaginal wall. Such lesions can be multiple. There is no risk for malignancy. A Bartholin gland (greater vestibular gland) cyst may arise in the vagina, just inside the entrance in the posterior vestibule.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Gonorrhea is more likely to produce a urethritis, cervicitis, or pelvic inflammatory disease. It is unlikely to produce a mass lesion of the vagina.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Carcinomas are uncommon at her age. The vagina is an uncommon site for metastases.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Oral contraceptive use does not lead to vaginal mass lesions.
  Choice F: Incorrect. Objects inserted are likely to be large and smooth-surfaced, though local trauma with abrasion or contusion is possible.

QUESTION 30 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q30]

----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 33-year-old woman has an infertility workup. Her BMI is 32 kg/m2. Hirsutism is noted. On pelvic examination, bilateral adnexal masses are palpated. Transvaginal ultrasound examination shows a normal sized uterus, but the ovaries are enlarged and have a dozen peripheral echogenic follicles. Laboratory studies show hyperglycemia and increased androgenic steroids. For which of the following conditions is she at greatest risk?

CHOICES:
  A. Endometrial hyperplasia <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  B. Adenomyosis
  C. Carcinosarcoma
  D. Chronic endometritis
  E. Borderline mucinous tumor

CORRECT ANSWER: A. Endometrial hyperplasia

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  She has polycystic ovarian syndrome (PCOS), and the enlarged ovaries reflect abnormal hormonal balance that leads to anovulation and infertility. Obesity with insulin resistance and diabetes mellitus often accompany PCOS.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: A. Endometrial hyperplasia

CORRECT. She has polycystic ovarian syndrome (PCOS), and the enlarged ovaries reflect abnormal hormonal balance that leads to anovulation and infertility. Obesity with insulin resistance and diabetes mellitus often accompany PCOS. The hormonal excess drives endometrial hyperplasia, which can be atypical and progress to carcinoma.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: CORRECT. She has polycystic ovarian syndrome (PCOS), and the enlarged ovaries reflect abnormal hormonal balance that leads to anovulation and infertility. Obesity with insulin resistance and diabetes mellitus often accompany PCOS. The hormonal excess drives endometrial hyperplasia, which can be atypical and progress to carcinoma.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Adenomyosis does not develop as a result of PCOS.
  Choice C: Incorrect. A 3M tumor may have an epithelial component, but it is not related to ovarian abnormalities.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Chronic endometritis is not associated with malignancies, but the uterine bleeding that could occur must be distinguished from that caused by a carcinoma.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Serous and mucinous epithelial tumors of the ovary are not related to PCOS.

QUESTION 31 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q31]
Category: Gestational & Obstetric Pathology | Subtopic: Gestational & Obstetric Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 24-year-old woman is G2 P1. Her first pregnancy resulted in a normal term birth. Now at 15 weeks gestation, a prenatal checkup reveals that she has a blood pressure of 140/90 mm Hg. An ultrasound is performed that reveals no fetal cardiac motion. Misoprostol induction is performed for termination of the pregnancy. Examination of the malformed stillborn fetus reveals that it is small for gestational age and has 3,4 syndactyly bilaterally, an indented nasal bridge, and a two vessel cord. The placenta is small for gestation and has scattered 0.5 cm grape-like villi. A chromosome analysis performed on the placental tissue will most likely demonstrate which of the following karyotypes?

CHOICES:
  A. 46, XX
  B. 69, XXY <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  C. 45, X
  D. 47, XX, +18
  E. 23, X
  F. 47, XYY
  G. 47, XXX

CORRECT ANSWER: B. 69, XXY

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  This is triploidy. In a partial hydatidiform mole, a fetus is present but malformed and small for gestational age.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: B. 69, XXY

CORRECT. This is triploidy. In a partial hydatidiform mole, a fetus is present but malformed and small for gestational age. Only occasional villi, or none at all, may be grape-like, but there is trophoblastic proliferation typical of molar pregnancy. A partial mole pregnancy is unlikely to go to term, and it is unlikely to progress to choriocarcinoma.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. The 46, XX karyotype would be appropriate for a normal female fetus. It could also be seen with a complete mole in which no fetus would be present.
  Choice B: CORRECT. This is triploidy. In a partial hydatidiform mole, a fetus is present but malformed and small for gestational age. Only occasional villi, or none at all, may be grape-like, but there is trophoblastic proliferation typical of molar pregnancy. A partial mole pregnancy is unlikely to go to term, and it is unlikely to progress to choriocarcinoma.
  Choice C: Incorrect. The 45, X karyotype is seen with Turner syndrome, one of the more common chromosomal abnormalities seen with fetal losses. It is not accompanied by placental changes of molar pregnancy.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Trisomy 18 leads to an abnormal appearing fetus with clenched hands (not syndactyly), and the placenta may be small, but does not show changes of molar pregnancy.
  Choice E: Incorrect. The 23, X karyotype is that of an unfertilized ovum or a sperm.
  Choice F: Incorrect. This karyotype is indicative of Klinfelter syndrome, seen in nearly normal appearing males with oligospermia.
  Choice G: Incorrect. This is the 'superfemale' karyotype with only mild intellectual disability.

STEM:
A 16-year-old girl has not begun menstruation. Physical examination reveals that she has breast development, but a short vagina and no palpable uterus or adnexa, only bilateral inguinal masses. She appears otherwise normally developed. Which of the following laboratory tests would be most appropriate to order on this girl?

  D. Serum cortisol
  E. Serum testosterone
  F. Magnetic resonance imaging of brain

  She has primary amenorrhea. The findings point to the rare testicular feminization (androgen insensitivity syndrome), with a karyotype of 46, XY.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: A. Chromosome analysis

CORRECT. She has primary amenorrhea. The findings point to the rare testicular feminization (androgen insensitivity syndrome), with a karyotype of 46, XY. The inguinal masses are the testes.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: CORRECT. She has primary amenorrhea. The findings point to the rare testicular feminization (androgen insensitivity syndrome), with a karyotype of 46, XY. The inguinal masses are the testes.
  Choice B: Incorrect. This would be more useful for workup of secondary amenorrhea.
  Choice C: Incorrect. This would be more useful for workup of secondary amenorrhea.

QUESTION 33 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q33]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 38-year-old healthy woman has had a white, curd-like vaginal discharge for the past week. There is no bleeding. A Pap smear demonstrates normal appearing squamous epithelial cells along with scattered neutrophils and budding cells with pseudohyphae. Which of the following infectious agents is most likely to be present in this woman?

CHOICES:
  A. <em>Treponema pallidum</em>
  B. <em>Neisseria gonorrhea</em>
  C. <em>Chlamydia trachomatis</em>
  D. Herpes simplex virus
  E. <em>Escherichia coli</em>
  F. <em>Gardnerella vaginalis</em>
  G. <em>Trichomonas vaginalis</em>
  H. <em>Candida albicans</em> <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  I. Human papillomavirus

CORRECT ANSWER: H. <em>Candida albicans</em>

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Vaginal yeast infections are quite common. Fungal vulvovaginitis is the second most common cause of vaginal infections in the U.S.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: H. Candida albicans

CORRECT. Vaginal yeast infections are quite common. Fungal vulvovaginitis is the second most common cause of vaginal infections in the U.S. (Bacterial vaginosis is the most frequent cause). The Candida organisms have the morphologic appearance with budding cells, often with pseudohyphae.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. This is the causative agent for syphilis, which as a primary lesion causes a hard chancre.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Gonorrhea is more of a cause for vaginitis in children.
  Choice C: Incorrect. <em>Chlamydia trachomatis</em> is a sexually transmitted disease that is unlikely to produce a marked vaginal exudate.
  Choice D: Incorrect. HSV infection leads to the recurrent appearance of crops of painful vesicles, typically on the external genitalia.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Gram negative enteric bacterial infections are not common at this site.
  Choice F: Bacterial vaginosis is caused by overgrowth of a variety of bacteria (<em>Gardnerella vaginalis</em>, <em>Prevotella</em>, <em>Mobiluncus</em>, <em>Peptostreptococcus</em>, <em>Mycoplasma hominis</em>, etc.) in the vagina. It differs from fungal vulvovaginitis in that (1) there is usually a rather unpleasant and quite noticeable 'fishy' odor; (2) the vaginal discharge is homogenous, lower in viscosity, and uniformly coats the vaginal walls; and (3) the vaginal pH is usually >4.5.
  Choice G: Incorrect. Trichomonas vulvovaginitis is caused by the protozoan <em>Trichomonas vaginalis</em>. It differs from fungal vulvovaginitis in that there is a profuse, homogenous vaginal discharge that usually has a yellow or greenish-yellow color. Vaginal pH is usually > 5.0, and the 'fishy' odor may or may not be present. Trichomonas vulvovaginitis is diagnosed by observation of motile trichomonads and large numbers of PMNs in a vaginal smear.
  Choice H: CORRECT. Vaginal yeast infections are quite common. Fungal vulvovaginitis is the second most common cause of vaginal infections in the U.S. (Bacterial vaginosis is the most frequent cause). The <em>Candida</em> organisms have the morphologic appearance with budding cells, often with pseudohyphae.
  Choice I: Incorrect. HPV is a cause for condyloma and for squamous dysplasias and carcinomas, not vaginosis.

QUESTION 35 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q35]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
Abnormal bleeding per vagina for the past 5 months prompts a 62-year-old woman to see her physician. She has never been pregnant and went through menopause 10 years previously. On physical examination her BMI is 33 kg/m2. There are no abnormal findings on physical examination. An endometrial biopsy is performed and on microscopic examination shows a well-differentiated endometrial adenocarcinoma. Which of the following ovarian neoplasms is she most likely to have?

CHOICES:
  A. Papillary serous cystadenocarcinoma
  B. Krukenberg tumor
  C. Mucinous cystadenoma
  D. Granulosa cell tumor <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  E. Immature teratoma

CORRECT ANSWER: D. Granulosa cell tumor

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  She could have an ovarian tumor producing estrogen, and the granulosa cell tumor is most likely to do that, of the ones listed. Her obesity is a risk factor for endometrial carcinoma as well.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: D. Granulosa cell tumor

CORRECT. She could have an ovarian tumor producing estrogen, and the granulosa cell tumor is most likely to do that, of the ones listed. Her obesity is a risk factor for endometrial carcinoma as well.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Cystadenocarcinomas are epithelial tumors unlikely to secrete estrogen.
  Choice B: Incorrect. A Krukenberg tumor represents metastatic disease of the ovary, most often from a gastrointestinal malignancy (such as primary gastric adenocarcinoma).
  Choice C: Incorrect. Cystadenomas, which are of epithelial origin, are not likely to secrete estrogen.
  Choice D: CORRECT. She could have an ovarian tumor producing estrogen, and the granulosa cell tumor is most likely to do that, of the ones listed. Her obesity is a risk factor for endometrial carcinoma as well.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Immature teratomas contain primitive elements with neural differentiation and are unlikely to secrete estrogens.

QUESTION 36 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q36]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 28-year-old woman has noted the appearance of multiple skin lesions in her genital region over the past month. The lesions are pruritic but do not bleed when scratched. On examination there are multiple papular 0.3 to 1 cm pearly, plaquelike to cauliflower lesions with a rough surface and color similar to surrounding vulvar skin. The development of these findings is most likely related to infection with which of the following organisms?

CHOICES:
  A. <em>Treponema pallidum</em>
  B. Human papillomavirus <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  C. <em>Neisseria gonorrheae</em>
  D. <em>Candida albicans</em>
  E. Group B <em>Streptococcus</em>

CORRECT ANSWER: B. Human papillomavirus

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  HPV is associated with increased risk for condyloma accuminatum. Such lesions are often multiple.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: B. Human papillomavirus

CORRECT. HPV is associated with increased risk for condyloma accuminatum. Such lesions are often multiple. HPV types 6 and 11 are the usual culprits, explaining why the HPV vaccine typically is multivalent to include these subtypes.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Syphilis can produce a chancre of the external genitalia or cervix.
  Choice B: CORRECT. HPV is associated with increased risk for condyloma accuminatum. Such lesions are often multiple. HPV types 6 and 11 are the usual culprits, explaining why the HPV vaccine typically is multivalent to include these subtypes.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Cases of pelvic inflammatory disease (PID) are usually due to gonorrhea or chlamydial infection, which are sexually transmitted diseases. The tubal scarring from the inflammation leads to an increased risk for ectopic pregnancy
  Choice D: Incorrect. <em>Candida</em> is a cause for vaginitis, not condylomata.
  Choice E: Incorrect. This organism presents the greatest risk as an intrauterine infection resulting in stillbirth in the third trimester.

QUESTION 37 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q37]
Category: Endometrial & Uterine Pathology | Subtopic: Endometrial & Uterine Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 44-year-old woman has had irregular menstrual periods for the past 9 months. On physical examination she has an enlarged uterus. An ultrasound scan shows a 9 cm solitary, solid mass in the uterus. A total abdominal hysterectomy is performed. Gross examination of the irregular reddish-tan mass located in the myometrium shows bundles of smooth muscle cells along with heterogenous elements of pleomorphic cartilaginous cells. There are also areas with poorly differentiated gland formation. Mitotic figures are frequent. Which of the following neoplasms is she most likely to have?

CHOICES:
  A. Leiomyosarcoma
  B. Sarcoma botryoides
  C. Carcinosarcoma <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  D. Endolymphatic stromal myosis
  E. Leiomyoma with focal degeneration

CORRECT ANSWER: C. Carcinosarcoma

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  A carcinosarcoma typically has malignant components that are both epithelial and stromal. The stromal component does not always resemble just smooth muscle but may have sarcomatous elements resembling other mesodermal tissues.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: C. Carcinosarcoma

CORRECT. A carcinosarcoma typically has malignant components that are both epithelial and stromal. The stromal component does not always resemble just smooth muscle but may have sarcomatous elements resembling other mesodermal tissues.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. The leiomyosarcoma is distinguished from a leiomyoma by the cellularity and the mitotic rate, but it is composed only of cells resembling smooth muscle.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Sarcoma botryoides is a rare neoplasm most likely to occur in the vagina of a child. It is composed of primitive cells most resembling an embryonal rhabdomyosarcoma.
  Choice C: CORRECT. A carcinosarcoma typically has malignant components that are both epithelial and stromal. The stromal component does not always resemble just smooth muscle but may have sarcomatous elements resembling other mesodermal tissues.
  Choice D: Incorrect. This rare condition consists of endometrial stromal cells extending into the body of uterus and beyond.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Some leiomyomas may undergo 'red degeneration' but a leiomyoma should not have a significant mitotic rate.

QUESTION 38 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q38]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 72-year-old woman has had fatigue for the past year, along with episodes of vaginal bleeding. On physical examination there are no abnormal findings. Laboratory studies show Hgb 9.1 g/dL, Hct 26.5%, MCV 72 fL, platelet count 158,000/microliter, and WBC count 7150/microliter. An endometrial biopsy is performed and on microscopic examination shows atypical adenomatous hyperplasia. Which of the following is the most likely risk factor for development of her disease?

CHOICES:
  A. Human papillomavirus infection
  B. Long term intrauterine contraceptive device use
  C. Chronic endometritis
  D. Pelvic inflammatory disease
  E. Unopposed estrogenic stimulation <-- CORRECT ANSWER

CORRECT ANSWER: E. Unopposed estrogenic stimulation

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  The unopposed estrogen drives the hyperplasia, which if atypical carries an increased risk for development of an endometrial adenocarcinoma.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: E. Unopposed estrogenic stimulation

CORRECT. The unopposed estrogen drives the hyperplasia, which if atypical carries an increased risk for development of an endometrial adenocarcinoma.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. HPV infection mainly produces lesions of squamous epithelium of external genitalia and cervix, such as dysplasias and carcinoma.
  Choice B: Incorrect. An IUD can cause endometritis, but not a preneoplastic lesion.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Chronic endometritis is not generally known as an antecedent to preneoplastic or neoplastic conditions.
  Choice D: Incorrect. PID can produce inflammation with scarring, mainly of tubes and ovaries.
  Choice E: CORRECT. The unopposed estrogen drives the hyperplasia, which if atypical carries an increased risk for development of an endometrial adenocarcinoma.

QUESTION 39 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q39]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 56-year-old G0 P0 woman reports vaginal bleeding in the past 2 months. Her last menstrual period was 6 years ago. On physical examination there are no abnormal findings. Which of the following procedures is most appropriate to perform on this woman?

CHOICES:
  A. Endometrial biopsy <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  B. Pap smear
  C. Vaginal culture
  D. Colposcopy
  E. CT scan

CORRECT ANSWER: A. Endometrial biopsy

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  This history points to a possible endometrial carcinoma, and nulliparity increases the risk.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: A. Endometrial biopsy

CORRECT. This history points to a possible endometrial carcinoma, and nulliparity increases the risk.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: CORRECT. This history points to a possible endometrial carcinoma, and nulliparity increases the risk.
  Choice B: Incorrect. CIN is less likely than an endometrial lesion in this setting.
  Choice C: Incorrect. An infection would be unlikely to cause bleeding.
  Choice D: Incorrect. This will show cervical lesions, not endometrial.
  Choice E: Incorrect. The possible causes of uterine bleeding cannot be distinguished by CT (or MRI) scan.

QUESTION 40 of 40 [ID: webpath-fem-q40]
Category: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology | Subtopic: Cervical, Vulvar & Vaginal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 19-year-old G2P2 woman delivered a 36 week gestational age infant 4 days ago following premature prolonged rupture of membranes. She now has fever, chills, and lower abdominal pain. On examination her temperature is 38 C. There is a foul smelling vaginal discharnge. Which of the following is the most likely causative microorganism?

CHOICES:
  A. <em>Candida albicans</em>
  B. Group B <em>Streptococcus</em> <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  C. Herpes simplex virus
  D. Human papillomavirus
  E. <em>Neisseria gonorrheae</em>
  F. <em>Treponema pallidum</em>

CORRECT ANSWER: B. Group B <em>Streptococcus</em>

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Group B <em>Streptococcus</em> (GBS) can be associated with acute postpartum endometritis. GBS can colonize the vagina.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: B. Group B Streptococcus

CORRECT. Group B Streptococcus (GBS) can be associated with acute postpartum endometritis. GBS can colonize the vagina. GBS infection can manifest in several ways: (1) stillbirth, often near or at term; (2) early onset infection in the neonate in first week of life; and (3) intrauterine infection following birth.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. <em>Candida albicans</em> is a cause for vaginitis, but is unlikely to ascend to the uterine cavity.
  Choice B: CORRECT. Group B <em>Streptococcus</em> (GBS) can be associated with acute postpartum endometritis. GBS can colonize the vagina. GBS infection can manifest in several ways: (1) stillbirth, often near or at term; (2) early onset infection in the neonate in first week of life; and (3) intrauterine infection following birth.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Herpes simplex virus can produce a vesicular eruption, and may be passed to the infant in the birth process.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Human papillomavirus is associated with squamous cell proliferations, but not acute infections.
  Choice E: Incorrect. <em>Neisseria gonorrheae</em> can produce pelvic inflammatory disease, which reduces fertility. It is not a common cause for acute endometritis postpartum.
  Choice F: Incorrect. <em>Treponema pallidum</em> can be associated with formation of a chancre, but is not associated with acute postpartum endometritis.
