QUESTION 1 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q1]
Category: Testicular & Scrotal Pathology | Subtopic: Testicular & Scrotal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 25-year-old man and his wife undergo an infertility workup. His wife's reproductive function is normal. On physical examination he has descended testes that appear decreased in size. A sperm count shows aspermia. A testicular biopsy is performed and on microscopic examination only Sertoli cells are present in the seminiferous tubules. Which of the following laboratory test findings is he most likely to have?

CHOICES:
  A. Increased FSH <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  B. Increased HCG
  C. Increased alpha-fetoprotein
  D. Decreased testosterone
  E. Decreased muellerian inhibiting substance
  F. Decreased luteinizing hormone

CORRECT ANSWER: A. Increased FSH

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Such a 'Sertoli cell only' syndrome is a cause for male infertility and may be idiopathic. A similar pattern would be present in a cryptorchid testis.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: A. Increased FSH

CORRECT. Such a 'Sertoli cell only' syndrome is a cause for male infertility and may be idiopathic. A similar pattern would be present in a cryptorchid testis.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: CORRECT. Such a 'Sertoli cell only' syndrome is a cause for male infertility and may be idiopathic. A similar pattern would be present in a cryptorchid testis.
  Choice B: Incorrect. An elevated HCG in a male suggests the presence of testicular choriocarcinoma.
  Choice C: Incorrect. An elevation of AFP suggests the presence of a testicular neoplasm with a yolk sac element.
  Choice D: Incorrect. The Leydig cells of the testicular interstitium continue to function. He does not have loss of secondary sex characteristics.
  Choice E: Incorrect. MIF is a hormone active in embryogenesis of males which controls testicular descent in the first abdominal phase, causing the testes to come to lie low in the abdomen, in the second stage of descent the testes descend under control of androgens down the inguinal canals to the scrotum.
  Choice F: Incorrect. LH acts on the Leydig cells of the testicular interstitium, which are generally unaffected by processes that reduce the numbers of germ cells.


----------------------------------------------------------------------
QUESTION 2 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q2]
Category: Testicular & Scrotal Pathology | Subtopic: Testicular & Scrotal Pathology
Focus: Drugs & Management (Pharmacotherapy & Drug Selection)
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 30-year-old man has had a feeling of heaviness in his left testis for the past 6 months. Physical examination reveals enlargement of the left testis, while the right testis appears normal. There is a palpable left inguinal lymph node. An ultrasound reveals a 4 cm solid mass within the body of the left testis. Laboratory findings included a serum beta-HCG of 5 IU/L and alpha-fetoprotein of 2 ng/mL. The left testis is removed and with on sectioning reveals a firm, lobulated light tan mass without hemorrhage or necrosis. He receives radiation therapy. Which of the following neoplasms is he most likely to have?

CHOICES:
  A. Choriocarcinoma
  B. Embryonal carcinoma
  C. Seminoma <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  D. Yolk sac tumor
  E. Leydig cell tumor

CORRECT ANSWER: C. Seminoma

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  The most common pure form of testicular cancer is seminoma, a type of germ cell tumor which is radiosensitive. The tumor markers are not markedly elevated.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: C. Seminoma

CORRECT. The most common pure form of testicular cancer is seminoma, a type of germ cell tumor which is radiosensitive. The tumor markers are not markedly elevated. This form of testicular carcinoma has the best prognosis overall, when not mixed with other elements.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Choriocarcinomas are very aggressive tumors. Grossly they can be soft and hemorrhagic. A high HCG would suggest a component of choriocarcinoma.
  Choice B: Incorrect. This tumor does not respond as well to chemotherapy as seminoma. A high AFP would suggest embryonal carcinoma or yolk sac carcinoma.
  Choice C: CORRECT. The most common pure form of testicular cancer is seminoma, a type of germ cell tumor which is radiosensitive. The tumor markers are not markedly elevated. This form of testicular carcinoma has the best prognosis overall, when not mixed with other elements.
  Choice D: Incorrect. This is the most common testicular neoplasm in males under age 3. In adults, yolk sac elements are mixed with embryonal carcinoma.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Leydig cell tumors are not common. This benign tumor is usually small in size.

QUESTION 3 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q3]
Category: Testicular & Scrotal Pathology | Subtopic: Testicular & Scrotal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 35-year-old man goes to his physician for a routine examination. On physical examination there is a left inguinal mass. The right testis is palpated in the scrotum and is of normal size, but a left testis cannot be palpated in the scrotum. An ultrasound scan shows that there is a 2 cm solid inguinal mass. Which of the following approaches is most appropriate to deal with this patient's testicular abnormality?

CHOICES:
  A. Put the mass into the scrotum surgically
  B. Remove the mass along with the opposite testis
  C. Remove the mass <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  D. Put the patient on testosterone therapy
  E. Put the patient on chemotherapy

CORRECT ANSWER: C. Remove the mass

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  A cryptorchid testis that is not treated in early childhood no longer functions in spermatogenesis and presents a risk for subsequent development of seminoma. The earlier in life that an orchiopexy is performed, generally under the age of 5, the more likely the testis will function properly.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: C. Remove the mass

CORRECT. A cryptorchid testis that is not treated in early childhood no longer functions in spermatogenesis and presents a risk for subsequent development of seminoma. The earlier in life that an orchiopexy is performed, generally under the age of 5, the more likely the testis will function properly. If the opposite testis were also cryptorchid, it would be at increased risk for development of carcinoma. However, the risk of carcinoma in the descended testis is unlikely to be increased, so it can be left to function. The patient may opt for orchiopexy of the cryptorchid testis, but it can be explained that fertility and hormone production can be provided by the descended testis. If the patient wanted two 'balls' in the scrotum, a prosthesis could be placed.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. The testis has been out of place too long to retain any function of spermatogenesis. It is now atrophic with few remaining germ cells and minimal, if any, spermatogenesis occurring. There is a risk for carcinoma.
  Choice B: Incorrect. The opposite testis will not be affected by cancer risk present in the cryptorchid testis. Removing the descended testis removes fertility and hormone production.
  Choice C: CORRECT. A cryptorchid testis that is not treated in early childhood no longer functions in spermatogenesis and presents a risk for subsequent development of seminoma. The earlier in life that an orchiopexy is performed, generally under the age of 5, the more likely the testis will function properly. If the opposite testis were also cryptorchid, it would be at increased risk for development of carcinoma. However, the risk of carcinoma in the descended testis is unlikely to be increased, so it can be left to function. The patient may opt for orchiopexy of the cryptorchid testis, but it can be explained that fertility and hormone production can be provided by the descended testis. If the patient wanted two 'balls' in the scrotum, a prosthesis could be placed.
  Choice D: Incorrect. The patient still has one good testis. Even with bilateral cryptorchidism, the Leydig cells of the testicular interstitium continue to function and produce testosterone.
  Choice E: Incorrect. This mass is likely the undescended testis; it is unlikely to have a carcinoma, though there is increasing risk over time.


----------------------------------------------------------------------
QUESTION 4 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q4]
Category: Prostate Pathology & Neoplasms | Subtopic: Prostate Pathology & Neoplasms
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 70-year-old healthy man has a routine check-up. On physical examination there is a firm nodule palpable in the prostate via digital rectal examination. A magnetic resonance imaging scan confirms the presence and location of a posterior 0.4 cm nodule. Prostate biopsies are performed and on microscopic examination show small, crowded glands containing cells with prominent nucleoli within the nuclei. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Adenocarcinoma <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  B. Nodular hyperplasia
  C. Chronic prostatitis
  D. Urothelial carcinoma
  E. Recent infarction

CORRECT ANSWER: A. Adenocarcinoma

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Such a nodule at that age strongly suggests carcinoma. Most carcinomas of the prostate arise in the posterior portion of the gland where they can be palpated on digital rectal examination.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: A. Adenocarcinoma

CORRECT. Such a nodule at that age strongly suggests carcinoma. Most carcinomas of the prostate arise in the posterior portion of the gland where they can be palpated on digital rectal examination. Microscopically, prostatic adenocarcinomas have irregular glands without intervening stroma. Large nucleoli are a characteristic microscopic feature.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: CORRECT. Such a nodule at that age strongly suggests carcinoma. Most carcinomas of the prostate arise in the posterior portion of the gland where they can be palpated on digital rectal examination. Microscopically, prostatic adenocarcinomas have irregular glands without intervening stroma. Large nucleoli are a characteristic microscopic feature.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Hyperplasia is usually not a focal process, and the glands are not small and crowded.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Inflammation is usually not focal in the prostate.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Urothelial carcinomas do not appear glandular.
  Choice E: Incorrect. The glands around an infarct may show squamous metaplasia, but they will not be small and crowded.

QUESTION 5 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q5]
Category: Penile & Male Urethral Pathology | Subtopic: Penile & Male Urethral Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
An epidemiologic study is performed to determine potential risk factors for development of penile squamous intraepithelial neoplasia. It is observed that persons who develop this disease are elderly men. The medical histories of these men are reviewed. Which of the following diseases is most likely to be found to precede development of penile neoplasia in these men?

CHOICES:
  A. Phimosis <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  B. Herpes simplex virus infection
  C. Lichen simplex chronicus
  D. Balanitis xerotica obliterans
  E. Epispadias

CORRECT ANSWER: A. Phimosis

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  The chronic irritation from accumulation of secretions and smegma under the prepuce is the likely risk. Circumcision reduces the risk for development of penile carcinoma, and can reduce transmission of infections as well.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: A. Phimosis

CORRECT. The chronic irritation from accumulation of secretions and smegma under the prepuce is the likely risk. Circumcision reduces the risk for development of penile carcinoma, and can reduce transmission of infections as well.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: CORRECT. The chronic irritation from accumulation of secretions and smegma under the prepuce is the likely risk. Circumcision reduces the risk for development of penile carcinoma, and can reduce transmission of infections as well.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Although herpes simplex virus infection can be a recurrent infection, it is not premalignant.
  Choice C: Incorrect. This is an eczematous dermatitis that is not premalignant.
  Choice D: Incorrect. This manifestation of lichen simplex may result in stricture of the urethral meatus.
  Choice E: Incorrect. This congenital condition results in a groove or a cleft on the dorsum of the penis into which the urethra opens.

QUESTION 6 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q6]
Category: Penile & Male Urethral Pathology | Subtopic: Penile & Male Urethral Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 32-year-old man reports increasing size and number of lesions in his genital region. On examination there are multiple 0.2 to 1 cm raised, smooth to rough-surfaced to verrucous pale pink plaques on the penis, scrotum, and perineum. No ulceration is observed. Which of the following organisms is most likely to result in his lesions?

CHOICES:
  A. <em>Chlamydia trachomatis</em>
  B. Human papillomavirus <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  C. <em>Klebsiella granulomatis</em>
  D. <em>Treponema pallidum</em>
  E. <em>Hemophilus ducreyi</em>
  F. Herpes simplex virus

CORRECT ANSWER: B. Human papillomavirus

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  HPV produces genital warts and cancers, but not typically ulcers.

  Correct Answer: B. Human papillomavirus

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:

  Choice B: CORRECT. HPV produces genital warts and cancers, but not typically ulcers.
  Choice C: Incorrect. This organism produces the chronic ulcers of granuloma inguinale.

QUESTION 7 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q7]
Category: Male Reproductive Pathology | Subtopic: Male Reproductive Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 23-year-old healthy man has been unable to father a child. He and his wife have a workup for infertility. His wife's reproductive function is normal. On physical examination both his testes are palpable in the scrotum and the testes and scrotum are normal in size, with no masses palpable. However, the spermatic cord on the left has the feel of a 'bag of worms'. Laboratory studies show oligospermia. Which of the following conditions is this man most likely to have?

CHOICES:
  A. Hydrocele
  B. Testicular torsion
  C. Spermatocele
  D. Varicocele <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  E. Seminoma

CORRECT ANSWER: D. Varicocele

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  The increased warmth from the vascularity reduces sperm production. Spermatogenesis needs to occur at a temperature below that of the body as a whole, which explains why testes are in the scrotum.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: D. Varicocele

CORRECT. The increased warmth from the vascularity reduces sperm production. Spermatogenesis needs to occur at a temperature below that of the body as a whole, which explains why testes are in the scrotum.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. A hydrocele may produce an enlargement that has smooth borders. Hydroceles generally do not affect the sperm count.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Torsion is an acute emergency because the arterial blood supply to the testis is cut off.
  Choice C: Incorrect. A spermatocele is usually a small circumscribed mass and does not affect the sperm count.
  Choice D: CORRECT. The increased warmth from the vascularity reduces sperm production. Spermatogenesis needs to occur at a temperature below that of the body as a whole, which explains why testes are in the scrotum.
  Choice E: Incorrect. A seminoma usually produces a firm mass lesion.

QUESTION 8 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q8]
Category: Prostate Pathology & Neoplasms | Subtopic: Prostate Pathology & Neoplasms
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 72-year-old man gets up several times during a football match to go to the restroom to urinate, even though he has had only one beer. This is a problem that has plagued him for 4 years. When he visits his physician for a checkup, on physical examination he has a diffusely enlarged prostate palpated on digital rectal examination. Laboratory studies show his serum prostate specific antigen is 6 ng/mL. Which of the following pathologic findings is most likely to be present in this man?

CHOICES:
  A. Adenocarcinoma
  B. Acute inflammation
  C. Multiple infarctions
  D. Nodular hyperplasia <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  E. Granulomas

CORRECT ANSWER: D. Nodular hyperplasia

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH) is a common finding in older men. The lateral lobes are typically involved (in contrast to carcinomas which are most often found in the posterior lobe), and the increasing prostatic urethral obstruction leads to the classic symptoms of frequency and hesitancy with urination.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: D. Nodular hyperplasia

CORRECT. Benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH) is a common finding in older men. The lateral lobes are typically involved (in contrast to carcinomas which are most often found in the posterior lobe), and the increasing prostatic urethral obstruction leads to the classic symptoms of frequency and hesitancy with urination. The PSA can be mildly elevated with hyperplasia, but the level tends not to increase significantly over time.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. An adenocarcinoma is more likely to be associated with a greater increase in the PSA level, and a carcinoma is more likely to be palpated as a solitary firm nodule.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Acute prostatitis can increase the PSA slightly and can produce some enlargement and pain, but his problem has persisted for several years.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Infarction of the prostate is not common, but can increase the PSA slightly. However, his problem has persisted for several years.
  Choice D: CORRECT. Benign prostatic hyperplasia (BPH) is a common finding in older men. The lateral lobes are typically involved (in contrast to carcinomas which are most often found in the posterior lobe), and the increasing prostatic urethral obstruction leads to the classic symptoms of frequency and hesitancy with urination. The PSA can be mildly elevated with hyperplasia, but the level tends not to increase significantly over time.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Granulomatous diseases are rare in the prostate.


----------------------------------------------------------------------
QUESTION 9 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q9]
Category: Testicular & Scrotal Pathology | Subtopic: Testicular & Scrotal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 19-year-old university student notes the sudden onset of severe discomfort in his scrotum late one evening. No position is comfortable for him to sit or lie down. Aspirin and beer have no effect. He has a friend drive him to the emergency room. On physical examination his vital signs include temperature 37°C, respirations 22/minute, pulse 80/minute, and blood pressure 100/65 mm Hg. His left testis is slightly enlarged and exquisitely tender. There is no inguinal adenopathy. A doppler ultrasound scan shows decreased blood flow in the left testis. Which of the following conditions is he most likely to have?

CHOICES:
  A. Choriocarcinoma
  B. Varicocele
  C. Lithiasis
  D. Torsion <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  E. Hydrocele

CORRECT ANSWER: D. Torsion

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Testicular torsion often has a sudden onset and you must recognize this condition, because it must be treated immediately, for the lack of blood flow can lead to hemorrhagic infarction. An ultrasound can be used to help identify this condition, demonstrating lack of blood flow.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: D. Torsion

CORRECT. Testicular torsion often has a sudden onset and you must recognize this condition, because it must be treated immediately, for the lack of blood flow can lead to hemorrhagic infarction. An ultrasound can be used to help identify this condition, demonstrating lack of blood flow.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Testicular neoplasms have a peak incidence in young adult males, not children.
  Choice B: Incorrect. This lesion does not typically produce sudden pain.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Urinary tract lithiasis can produce lower abdominal pain, but does not involve the testis.
  Choice D: CORRECT. Testicular torsion often has a sudden onset and you must recognize this condition, because it must be treated immediately, for the lack of blood flow can lead to hemorrhagic infarction. An ultrasound can be used to help identify this condition, demonstrating lack of blood flow.
  Choice E: Incorrect. A hydrocele slowly enlarges; such a lesion is more typical in adults.

QUESTION 10 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q10]
Category: Penile & Male Urethral Pathology | Subtopic: Penile & Male Urethral Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 20-year-old man has noted a penile discharge with some pain on urination for the last 2 days. On physical examination there is a small amount of whitish exudate that can be expressed from the urethral meatus. Laboratory studies with culture of the penile discharge reveal Neisseria gonorrheae. If untreated, which of the following complications is he most likely to develop as a consequence of his disease?

CHOICES:
  A. Aortitis
  B. Balanitis
  C. Epididymitis <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  D. Orchitis
  E. Sacroiliitis

CORRECT ANSWER: C. Epididymitis

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  When the testis is involved by gonorrhea, it is typically the epididymis. Many male gonorrheal infections are asymptomatic and not followed by significant complications.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: C. Epididymitis

CORRECT. When the testis is involved by gonorrhea, it is typically the epididymis. Many male gonorrheal infections are asymptomatic and not followed by significant complications. Urethritis with stricture is a possible complication.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. This is a complication of tertiary syphilis.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Gonorrhea tends to involve the urethra and epididymis and not the overlying skin.
  Choice C: CORRECT. When the testis is involved by gonorrhea, it is typically the epididymis. Many male gonorrheal infections are asymptomatic and not followed by significant complications. Urethritis with stricture is a possible complication.
  Choice D: Incorrect. The tubules and interstitium of the testis are typically not involved by gonorrhea, and infertility is unlikely.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Sacroiliitis is most often a feature of the spondyloarthropathies such as ankylosing spondylitis, or even reactive Arthritis resulting from a non-gonococcal urethritis such as <em>Chlamydia trachomatis</em> infection.

QUESTION 11 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q11]
Category: Testicular & Scrotal Pathology | Subtopic: Testicular & Scrotal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 31-year-old man has had a feeling of heaviness in his scrotum for over 6 months. On exam he has an enlarged right testis. An ultrasound reveals a solid 5 cm mass in the body of the right testis. Laboratory studies show a serum alpha-fetoprotein (AFP) of 81 ng/mL and human chorionic gonadotrophin (HCG) of 15 IU/L. A right orchiectomy is performed, and on gross examination the testicular mass is soft and reddish brown. Microscopic examination shows cords and sheets of primitive cells with large nuclei. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Teratoma
  B. Embryonal carcinoma <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  C. Mumps orchitis
  D. Leydig cell tumor
  E. Squamous cell carcinoma
  F. Choriocarcinoma

CORRECT ANSWER: B. Embryonal carcinoma

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  The embryonal carcinoma is likely to have an elevated AFP. Many malignant testicular neoplasms produce some detectable HCG, but this does not mean that choriocarcinoma is present.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: B. Embryonal carcinoma

CORRECT. The embryonal carcinoma is likely to have an elevated AFP. Many malignant testicular neoplasms produce some detectable HCG, but this does not mean that choriocarcinoma is present.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. A pure teratoma in the testis is very uncommon. Usually a malignant component such as embryonal carcinoma is present.
  Choice B: CORRECT. The embryonal carcinoma is likely to have an elevated AFP. Many malignant testicular neoplasms produce some detectable HCG, but this does not mean that choriocarcinoma is present.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Mumps orchitis may produce some residual patchy tubular atrophy of no real significance.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Leydig cell tumors are not common. Most are small brown masses.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Squamous cell carcinomas do not arise in the testis.
  Choice F: Incorrect. Choriocarcinomas have primitive appearing syncytiotrophoblastic cells in sheets with abundant hemorrhage. The HCG level is typically quite high.

QUESTION 12 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q12]
Category: Prostate Pathology & Neoplasms | Subtopic: Prostate Pathology & Neoplasms
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 77-year-old man has a routine check-up by his physician. The only physical examination finding is slight nodularity of his prostate on digital rectal examination. Laboratory studies show a serum prostate specific antigen of 6 ng/mL. A prostate biopsy is performed and on microscopic examination shows prostatic intraepithelial neoplasia (PIN). Which of the following is the best medical care option to offer this man?

CHOICES:
  A. Radical prostatectomy
  B. Multiagent chemotherapy
  C. Transurethral prostate resection
  D. Monitoring PSA levels <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  E. Nothing

CORRECT ANSWER: D. Monitoring PSA levels

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  PIN is a potential precursor of prostatic adenocarcinoma, but by itself does not warrant therapy.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: D. Monitoring PSA levels

CORRECT. PIN is a potential precursor of prostatic adenocarcinoma, but by itself does not warrant therapy.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. PIN is not life-threatening, but a precursor to prostatic adenocarcinoma that may require therapy later.
  Choice B: Incorrect. PIN can lead to prostate cancer later, but is not a condition that is treated.
  Choice C: Incorrect. PIN, by itself does not produce enlargement or nodules and does not warrant therapy. It may be found focally in the 'chips' from a TUR that was done for obstruction.
  Choice D: CORRECT. PIN is a potential precursor of prostatic adenocarcinoma, but by itself does not warrant therapy.
  Choice E: Incorrect. PIN is a precursor to prostate cancer and should not be completely ignored.

Category: Prostate Pathology & Neoplasms | Subtopic: Prostate Pathology & Neoplasms

STEM:
A clinical study is performed with subjects who are sexually active 30 to 45-year-old men. A subset of these subjects are found to have a history of pelvic pain with enlarged, tender prostates on digital rectal examination. However, routine microbiologic cultures from prostatic secretions in this subset of men show no growth. CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. This virus is associated with squamous epithelial changes, not with prostatitis.
  Choice B: Incorrect. HSV is associated with genital vesicles, not with prostatitis.

QUESTION 15 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q15]
Category: Penile & Male Urethral Pathology | Subtopic: Penile & Male Urethral Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 30-year-old sexually active man has experienced a burning pain with urination for the past 5 days. On physical examination there is a profuse yellowish urethral discharge obtained by milking the urethra along the shaft of the penis. He is afebrile. He is treated with ceftriaxone. He is most likely to be infected with which of the following organisms?

CHOICES:
  A. Herpes simplex virus
  B. <em>Treponema pallidum</em>
  C. <em>Neisseria gonorrheae</em> <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  D. Mumps virus
  E. <em>Hemophilus ducreyi</em>

CORRECT ANSWER: C. <em>Neisseria gonorrheae</em>

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Gonorrhea is a common cause urethritis in males. An acute urethritis is described.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: C. Neisseria gonorrheae

CORRECT. Gonorrhea is a common cause urethritis in males. An acute urethritis is described. Culture of the purulent discharge, but microbial culture of this fastidious organism is challenging, so molecular methods such as a DNA probe or PCR can be done for diagnosis.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. It would be unusual for HSV, which typically affects skin and mucus membranes, to involve the urethra in this fashion.
  Choice B: Incorrect. This is the causative agent for syphilis, which can manifest with a hard chancre on skin and mucus membranes.
  Choice C: CORRECT. Gonorrhea is a common cause urethritis in males. An acute urethritis is described. Culture of the purulent discharge, but microbial culture of this fastidious organism is challenging, so molecular methods such as a DNA probe or PCR can be done for diagnosis.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Mumps virus infection can produce a patchy orchitis, but does not cause urethritis.
  Choice E: Incorrect. This organism is associated with chancroid (soft chancre).

QUESTION 16 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q16]
Category: Penile & Male Urethral Pathology | Subtopic: Penile & Male Urethral Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 22-year-old G2 P1 woman gives birth following an uncomplicated pregnancy to a term male infant weighing 2850 gm. On physical examination he has incomplete development of the dorsal aspect of the penile urethra, with the defect extending to the bladder, which is open onto the lower abdominal wall. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Hypospadias
  B. Bowen disease
  C. Balanoposthitis
  D. Epispadias <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  E. Paraphimosis

CORRECT ANSWER: D. Epispadias

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Epispadias is a rare congenital anomaly that may be mild or, as in this case, severe with a large open defect that must be repaired. The failure to close the bladder is termed exstrophy.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: D. Epispadias

CORRECT. Epispadias is a rare congenital anomaly that may be mild or, as in this case, severe with a large open defect that must be repaired. The failure to close the bladder is termed exstrophy.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Hypospadias is an open defect involving the ventral aspect of the penile urethra.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Bowen disease is carcinoma in situ, a neoplastic process not seen in newborns.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Balanoposthitis is inflammation of the glans and foreskin, whether anomalous or not.
  Choice D: CORRECT. Epispadias is a rare congenital anomaly that may be mild or, as in this case, severe with a large open defect that must be repaired. The failure to close the bladder is termed exstrophy.
  Choice E: Incorrect. This results from forcible retraction of a phimotic prepuce, leading to constriction with urethral obstruction.

QUESTION 17 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q17]
Category: Male Reproductive Pathology | Subtopic: Male Reproductive Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 35-year-old healthy man desires a permanent form of contraception. He has a vasectomy performed with no complications of the surgery. He is advised of the potential future complications of this procedure. Which of the following is most likely to result from prior vasectomy?

CHOICES:
  A. Varicocele
  B. Orchitis
  C. Hydrocele
  D. Testicular torsion
  E. Sperm granuloma <-- CORRECT ANSWER

CORRECT ANSWER: E. Sperm granuloma

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Also called vasitis nodosa, a sperm granuloma results from a foreign body granulomatous reaction to extravasated sperm at the vasectomy site. The granulomatous reaction is typically not florid.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: E. Sperm granuloma

CORRECT. Also called vasitis nodosa, a sperm granuloma results from a foreign body granulomatous reaction to extravasated sperm at the vasectomy site. The granulomatous reaction is typically not florid.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. A varicocele, an abnormality of the pampiniform plexus sof veins, is not a complication of vasectomy. In fact, varicocels can be a cause for infertility, and he would not be getting a vasectomy.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Orchitis is an infectious process; vasectomy after healing from the surgery does not predispose to infection.
  Choice C: Incorrect. A hydrocele is a scrotal enlargement from increased fluid around the testis. Though there can be some local pain and edema following vasectomy, this subsides.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Torsion is a rare but serious complication of twisting of the testis; this does not follow vasectomy.
  Choice E: CORRECT. Also called vasitis nodosa, a sperm granuloma results from a foreign body granulomatous reaction to extravasated sperm at the vasectomy site. The granulomatous reaction is typically not florid.

QUESTION 18 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q18]

----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A study is conducted to document testicular abnormalities in adult male patients with no major medical problems who had biopsies performed for infertility workups. In some of these cases, the patients have normal sized testes but microscopic examination showing a patchy pattern of atrophy of testicular tubules. Which of the following infections is most likely to produce the findings seen in these men?

CHOICES:
  A. Human papillomavirus
  B. <em>Chlamydia trachomatis</em>
  C. <em>Neisseria gonorrheae</em>
  D. Mumps virus <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  E. Herpes simplex virus

CORRECT ANSWER: D. Mumps virus

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  This is a very common childhood infection (when vaccinations are not done) resulting in orchitis as well as parotitis. The inflammation rarely causes enough damage to produce a significantly reduced sperm count, if a childhood infection.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: D. Mumps virus

CORRECT. This is a very common childhood infection (when vaccinations are not done) resulting in orchitis as well as parotitis. The inflammation rarely causes enough damage to produce a significantly reduced sperm count, if a childhood infection. The virus tends to produce more testicular damage when adults are infected.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. HPV affects squamous epithelium, increasing the risk for dysplasias and carcinoma, but HPV does not affect the testis.
  Choice B: Incorrect. <em>Chlamydia</em> is a common cause for urethritis.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Gonorrhea may affect the epididymis, but it is less likely to affect the testis in a patchy pattern.
  Choice D: CORRECT. This is a very common childhood infection (when vaccinations are not done) resulting in orchitis as well as parotitis. The inflammation rarely causes enough damage to produce a significantly reduced sperm count, if a childhood infection. The virus tends to produce more testicular damage when adults are infected.
  Choice E: Incorrect. HSV affects the skin of the external male genitalia.

QUESTION 20 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q20]
Category: Testicular & Scrotal Pathology | Subtopic: Testicular & Scrotal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 40-year-old man has noted gradual enlargement of his scrotum, more on the right side, for the past 2 years. There is no associated pain, but the size is becoming uncomfortable. Physical examination reveals that the right side of the scrotum is enlarged to three times the size of the testis palpable on the left. This mass transilluminates. There is no tenderness on palpation. There is no inguinal lymphadenopathy. An ultrasound reveals a 5 cm thin-walled cystic fluid-filled area in the region of the right testis. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Seminoma
  B. Torsion
  C. Hydrocele <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  D. Varicocele
  E. Orchitis

CORRECT ANSWER: C. Hydrocele

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  A hydrocele is just a fluid filled sac that gradually enlarges. It represents fluid collection between the parietal and visceral layers of the tunica vaginalis.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: C. Hydrocele

CORRECT. A hydrocele is just a fluid filled sac that gradually enlarges. It represents fluid collection between the parietal and visceral layers of the tunica vaginalis. If congenital, it is the result of failure of the processus vaginalis to obliterate.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. A seminoma can produce a mass lesion with testicular enlargement, but is solid and does not transilluminate.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Torsion is uncommon but can occur in younger males and is associated with onset of severe, sudden pain.
  Choice C: CORRECT. A hydrocele is just a fluid filled sac that gradually enlarges. It represents fluid collection between the parietal and visceral layers of the tunica vaginalis. If congenital, it is the result of failure of the processus vaginalis to obliterate.
  Choice D: Incorrect. A varicocele is an enlarged mass of engorged veins in the pampiniform plexus. It is implicated in about a third of cases of male infertility.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Orchitis involves the body of the testis and is likely to be painful on palpation.

QUESTION 22 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q22]
Category: Prostate Pathology & Neoplasms | Subtopic: Prostate Pathology & Neoplasms
Focus: Drugs & Management (Pharmacotherapy & Drug Selection)
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 55-year-old man has experienced pain with urination for the past week. On physical examination his prostate is slightly enlarged and mildly tender to palpation on digital rectal examination. His temperature is 37.5°C. Laboratory studies show is WBC count is 12,910/microliter. Urine culture grows >100,000/mL Escherichia coli. His serum prostate specific antigen is 7 ng/mL. He receives antibiotic therapy and improves. This condition recurs 5 times in the next 6 months. Which of the following diseases is he most likely to have?

CHOICES:
  A. Nodular prostatic hyperplasia
  B. Prostatic adenocarcinoma
  C. Chronic bacterial prostatitis <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  D. Urothelial carcinoma
  E. Prostodynia

CORRECT ANSWER: C. Chronic bacterial prostatitis

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Chronic bacterial prostatitis should be considered in men who have a history of recurrent bacteriuria. A chronically infected prostate can serve as the source of pathogens for recurrent UTIs.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: C. Chronic bacterial prostatitis

CORRECT. Chronic bacterial prostatitis should be considered in men who have a history of recurrent bacteriuria. A chronically infected prostate can serve as the source of pathogens for recurrent UTIs. A pattern of relapsing UTIs in a middle-aged man strongly suggests chronic bacterial prostatitis. E. coli is a typical organism associated with urinary tract infection. The PSA can be slightly elevated with prostatic inflammation.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Prostatic hyperplasia can produce an enlarged prostate, but usually non-tender. The obstruction caused by an enlarged prostate can predispose to recurrent urinary tract infection.
  Choice B: Incorrect. The PSA can be slightly elevated with nodular hyperplasia. The PSA is typically elevated to more than twice normal when prostatic adenocarcinoma is present. The presence of a palpable nodule suggests a carcinoma. Prostatic adenocarcinomas are usually non-tender.
  Choice C: CORRECT. Chronic bacterial prostatitis should be considered in men who have a history of recurrent bacteriuria. A chronically infected prostate can serve as the source of pathogens for recurrent UTIs. A pattern of relapsing UTIs in a middle-aged man strongly suggests chronic bacterial prostatitis. <em>E. coli</em> is a typical organism associated with urinary tract infection. The PSA can be slightly elevated with prostatic inflammation.
  Choice D: Incorrect. A urothelial carcinoma at this location is uncommon. Hematuria is often present. It may cause obstruction and predispose to urinary tract infection.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Patients who have signs and symptoms of prostatitis but who have no evidence of prostatic inflammation (normal leukocyte counts) and have negative urine cultures are classified as having prostodynia.

QUESTION 23 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q23]

Focus: Drugs & Management (Pharmacotherapy & Drug Selection)
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 95-year-old man has had difficulty with urination for the past 15 years. On examination his prostate is diffusely enlarged. A transurethral resection is performed. On microscopic examination there is glandular hyperplasia and a focus of grade 1,2 adenocarcinoma. Which of the following is the next most appropriate treatment plan for this man?

CHOICES:
  A. Chemotherapy
  B. Alpha blocker therapy
  C. Radiotherapy
  D. Anti-androgen therapy
  E. No further therapy <-- CORRECT ANSWER

CORRECT ANSWER: E. No further therapy

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  A Gleason 1,2 adenocarcinoma is so low grade that it is unlikely to progress. At least half of men his age probably have a focus of prostatic adenocarcinoma.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: E. No further therapy

CORRECT. A Gleason 1,2 adenocarcinoma is so low grade that it is unlikely to progress. At least half of men his age probably have a focus of prostatic adenocarcinoma.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. The low grade carcinoma in this man is best left alone.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Alpha blockers are used to treat urinary retention from prostatic nodular hyperplasia.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Radiotherapy is not indicated for the low grade cancer in this man.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Anti-androgen therapy may be considered with more aggressive prostatic adenocarcinomas.
  Choice E: CORRECT. A Gleason 1,2 adenocarcinoma is so low grade that it is unlikely to progress. At least half of men his age probably have a focus of prostatic adenocarcinoma.

QUESTION 24 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q24]
Category: Penile & Male Urethral Pathology | Subtopic: Penile & Male Urethral Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A clinical study is performed to determine the optimal means for diagnosis of sexually transmitted diseases (STDs). The clinical and laboratory findings in subjects diagnosed with STDs are reviewed. A group of subjects is found to have Chlamydia trachomatis infection. Which of the following laboratory methods is most likely to be useful in diagnosis of C. trachomatis in these subjects?

CHOICES:
  A. Darkfield microscopic examination of exudate or secretions
  B. Cytologic smear
  C. Tissue biopsy
  D. Enzyme immunoassay <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  E. Microbiologic culture

CORRECT ANSWER: D. Enzyme immunoassay

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  This is the easiest and most cost-effective method, since <em>C. trachomatis</em> cannot be easily cultured by routine methods.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: D. Enzyme immunoassay

CORRECT. This is the easiest and most cost-effective method, since C. trachomatis cannot be easily cultured by routine methods.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. This is a good method for detection of spirochetes in scrapings from chancres in primary syphilis, but not for chlamydial infection.
  Choice B: Incorrect. A cytologic smear is useful for identifying viral cytopathic changes, typically for herpes simplex virus infection, but not for chlamydial infection.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Biopsy is typically not employed for diagnosis of STDs.
  Choice D: CORRECT. This is the easiest and most cost-effective method, since <em>C. trachomatis</em> cannot be easily cultured by routine methods.
  Choice E: Incorrect. <em>C. trachomatis</em> cannot be cultured by routine methods.

STEM:
A 33-year-old G3 P2 woman gives birth at term following an uncomplicated pregnancy to a male infant. On physical examination he has an abnormal opening of the urethra onto the ventral surface of the penis for a distance of 0.3 cm. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Hypospadias <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  B. Exstrophy
  C. Phimosis
  D. Epispadias
  E. Cryptorchidism

CORRECT ANSWER: A. Hypospadias

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  This is a congenital anomaly. Infection is a common complication, and partial urethral stricture may lead to urinary tract obstruction.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: A. Hypospadias

CORRECT. This is a congenital anomaly. Infection is a common complication, and partial urethral stricture may lead to urinary tract obstruction.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: CORRECT. This is a congenital anomaly. Infection is a common complication, and partial urethral stricture may lead to urinary tract obstruction.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Exstrophy is opening of the bladder through a defect in the lower abdominal wall.
  Choice C: Incorrect. This may be an anomaly, but it is more commonly due to inflammation with scarring in uncircumcised males.
  Choice D: Incorrect. This congenital anomaly is defined an abnormal opening of urethra on the dorsal aspect of the penis.
  Choice E: Incorrect. This condition is failure of normal descent of the testes into the scrotal sac. It occurs in about 1 male in 200.

Category: Penile & Male Urethral Pathology | Subtopic: Penile & Male Urethral Pathology

STEM:
A 45-year-old man complains of dysuria for the past week. On physical examination he is uncircumcised and has erythema and edema of the glans penis, with inability to retract the foreskin over the glans penis. Which of the following infectious agents is most likely to be associated with these findings?

CHOICES:
  A. Human papillomavirus
  B. <em>Staphylococcus aureus</em> <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  C. Herpes simplex virus
  D. <em>Treponema pallidum</em>
  E. <em>Sarcoptes scabiei</em>

CORRECT ANSWER: B. <em>Staphylococcus aureus</em>

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  He has balanitis with paraphimosis, and staphylococcal or streptococcal infections are most likely to be present.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: B. Staphylococcus aureus

CORRECT. He has balanitis with paraphimosis, and staphylococcal or streptococcal infections are most likely to be present.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. HPV can produce squamous epithelial condylomata, dysplasias, and carcinoma, typically without erythema and edema.
  Choice B: CORRECT. He has balanitis with paraphimosis, and staphylococcal or streptococcal infections are most likely to be present.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Herpetic infections typically produce vesicular lesions that are episodic in their recurrence.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Syphilis may produce the appearance of a hard chancre on the penis.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Scabies mites are typically a problem on the hands and less likely to produce itching burrows on the genitalia.

A pathologic study is performed in men ranging from 50 to 100 years of age who had serum prostate specific antigen levels above 15 ng/mL and who underwent prostatectomy following biopsies in which there was adenocarcinoma on microscopic examination. In which of the following regions of the prostate are these adenocarcinomas most likely to arise?

CHOICES:
  A. Anterior fibromuscular stroma
  B. Central zone

  E. Transitional zone

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: C. Peripheral zone

CORRECT. Most prostatic adenocarcinomas arise in the peripheral zone posteriorly where they can potentially be palpated by digital rectal examination.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Carcinomas are unlikely to arise in this location.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Carcinomas are unlikely to arise in this location.
  Choice C: CORRECT. Most prostatic adenocarcinomas arise in the peripheral zone posteriorly where they can potentially be palpated by digital rectal examination.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Carcinomas are unlikely to arise in this location, but hyperplasias often involve this zone.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Carcinomas are unlikely to arise in this location, but hyperplasias often involve this zone.

QUESTION 28 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q28]
Category: Penile & Male Urethral Pathology | Subtopic: Penile & Male Urethral Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 22-year-old man notes the presence of a lesion on his penis for the past week. On physical examination there is a solitary 0.7 cm diameter circumscribed area of ulceration on the dorsal aspect of his penis just proximal to the glans. This ulcer has a firm erythematous base containing minimal grey exudate. Which of the following is the most cost effective method for laboratory diagnosis of this man's lesion?

CHOICES:
  A. Darkfield microscopy <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  B. Cytologic smear
  C. Tissue biopsy
  D. Enzyme immunoassay
  E. Microbiologic culture

CORRECT ANSWER: A. Darkfield microscopy

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  The findings suggest a chancre of primary syphilis, and a scraping of the lesion put on a glass slide and examined by darkfield microscopy will allow identification of spirochetes within minutes. However, other than in STD clinics, a darkfield microscope is unlikely to be found.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: A. Darkfield microscopy

CORRECT. The findings suggest a chancre of primary syphilis, and a scraping of the lesion put on a glass slide and examined by darkfield microscopy will allow identification of spirochetes within minutes. However, other than in STD clinics, a darkfield microscope is unlikely to be found.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: CORRECT. The findings suggest a chancre of primary syphilis, and a scraping of the lesion put on a glass slide and examined by darkfield microscopy will allow identification of spirochetes within minutes. However, other than in STD clinics, a darkfield microscope is unlikely to be found.
  Choice B: Incorrect. A cytologic smear is best at identification of epithelial changes associated with herpes simplex virus infection. Herpes is likely to produce smaller and more numerous and painful ulcerations.
  Choice C: Incorrect. A tissue biopsy of a chancre will show numerous plasma cells, and a silver stain can highlight the spirochetes, but this is not a cost-effective way for diagnosis of syphilis.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Enzyme immunoassay is a good method for diagnosis of <em>Chlamydia trachomatis</em> infections, which are most likely to produce a urethritis.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Microbiologic culture is best suited to diagnosis of common bacterial pathogens, including <em>Neisseria gonorrheae</em>, which is unlikely to produce squamous epithelial ulcerations.

QUESTION 29 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q29]
Category: Testicular & Scrotal Pathology | Subtopic: Testicular & Scrotal Pathology
Focus: Drugs & Management (Pharmacotherapy & Drug Selection)
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A clinical study is conducted to determine the survival following treatment for testicular neoplasms utilizing subjects recorded into a hospital tumor registry. Treatments included surgery, chemotherapy, and radiation therapy. The patient records are reviewed to determine the pathologic diagnosis and the 5 year survivals for these patients. Which of the following types of testicular neoplasm is most likely to have responded best to radiation therapy?

CHOICES:
  A. Choriocarcinoma
  B. Embryonal carcinoma
  C. Seminoma <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  D. Teratoma
  E. Yolk sac tumor

CORRECT ANSWER: C. Seminoma

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Seminomas are the most radiosensitive of testicular carcinomas.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: C. Seminoma

CORRECT. Seminomas are the most radiosensitive of testicular carcinomas.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Choriocarcinomas are highly aggressive and require a combination of treatment methods.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Embryonal carcinomas are not highly radiosensitive.
  Choice C: CORRECT. Seminomas are the most radiosensitive of testicular carcinomas.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Pure teratomas of the testis are uncommon. The mature tissue elements of a teratoma are not highly radiosensitive.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Yolk sac tumors (infantile embryonal carcinomas) are rare, but they are the most common form of testicular tumor in children up to the age of 3.

QUESTION 30 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q30]
Category: Penile & Male Urethral Pathology | Subtopic: Penile & Male Urethral Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 35-year-old man has a routine check of his health status. On physical examination the prepuce cannot be fully retracted from the glans of his penis. No other abnormalities are noted. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Balanoposthitis
  B. Epispadias
  C. Exstrophy
  D. Hypospadias
  E. Phimosis <-- CORRECT ANSWER

CORRECT ANSWER: E. Phimosis

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Inflammation with scarring may prevent full retraction. There is an increased risk for phimosis and for balanitis in uncircumcised males.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: E. Phimosis

CORRECT. Inflammation with scarring may prevent full retraction. There is an increased risk for phimosis and for balanitis in uncircumcised males.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Balanoposthitis is inflammation of the glans and foreskin, which is marked by erythema, swelling, and exudate.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Epispadias is an abnormal dorsal urethral opening on the penis.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Exstrophy is opening of the bladder through a defect in the lower abdominal wall.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Hypospadias is an abnormal ventral urethral opening on the penis.
  Choice E: CORRECT. Inflammation with scarring may prevent full retraction. There is an increased risk for phimosis and for balanitis in uncircumcised males.

QUESTION 31 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q31]
Category: Male Reproductive Pathology | Subtopic: Male Reproductive Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A double blind study is conducted involving men who have been symptomatic from urinary frequency and hesitancy for 5 or more years. One group men receive a pharmacologic agent designed to reduce the synthesis of dihydrotestosterone in prostatic stromal cells. Another group receives a placebo. The group receiving the drug has a statistically significant decrease in symptoms. Which of the following enzymes is this pharmacologic agent most likely to block?

CHOICES:
  A. 5-alpha-reductase <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  B. Aromatase
  C. 17-alpha-hydroxylase
  D. Desmolase
  E. 11-beta-hydroxylase

CORRECT ANSWER: A. 5-alpha-reductase

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  Dihydrotestosterone (DHT) is synthesized from circulating testosterone by the action of 5-alpha-reductase type 2. It is DHT which binds to nuclear antigen receptors to stimulate cell growth and proliferation in the prostate.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: A. 5-alpha-reductase

CORRECT. Dihydrotestosterone (DHT) is synthesized from circulating testosterone by the action of 5-alpha-reductase type 2. It is DHT which binds to nuclear antigen receptors to stimulate cell growth and proliferation in the prostate.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: CORRECT. Dihydrotestosterone (DHT) is synthesized from circulating testosterone by the action of 5-alpha-reductase type 2. It is DHT which binds to nuclear antigen receptors to stimulate cell growth and proliferation in the prostate.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Estrogens are biosynthesized from androgens by the cytochrome P450 enzyme complex called aromatase. Aromatase inhibitors have been employed in control of breast cancers.
  Choice C: Incorrect. 17-alpha-hydroxlase is in the pathway from progesterone to hydroxyprogesterone. A deficiency of this enzyme reduces the amount of testosterone produced.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Desmolase is the enzyme involved in the initial step of steroid synthesis from cholesterol to pregnenolone.
  Choice E: Incorrect. 11-beta-hydroxylase is the enzyme involved with synthesis of both aldosterone and cortisol in the adrenal cortex.

QUESTION 32 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q32]
Category: Testicular & Scrotal Pathology | Subtopic: Testicular & Scrotal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 2-year-old boy is brought to the physician because his mother (a geometry teacher) has observed that his scrotum is no longer symmetrical. On physical examination the child has enlargement of the left testis. An ultrasound scan shows a 2 cm solid mass within the body of the testis. Laboratory studies show a serum alpha-fetoprotein of 226 ng/mL. Which of the following neoplasms is this child most likely to have?

CHOICES:
  A. Leydig cell tumor
  B. Neuroblastoma
  C. Rhabdomyosarcoma
  D. Teratoma
  E. Yolk sac tumor <-- CORRECT ANSWER

CORRECT ANSWER: E. Yolk sac tumor

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  The most common testicular tumor under the age of 3 is a yolk sac tumor (infantile embryonal carcinoma). However, finding any testicular tumor in children is uncommon.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: E. Yolk sac tumor

CORRECT. The most common testicular tumor under the age of 3 is a yolk sac tumor (infantile embryonal carcinoma). However, finding any testicular tumor in children is uncommon. The prognosis is good in most cases.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. A Leydig cell tumor is most often seen in adult men. It may produce androgens and/or estrogens.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Neuroblastoma is a childhood tumor most commonly arising in the adrenal.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Rhabdomyosarcoma is a childhood tumor that most often arises in the pelvis in the region of the prostate, or in the head & neck region.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Teratomas are second to yolk sac tumors in frequency as a testicular neoplasm. That still makes them rare. In adults, teratomas are rarely seen in a pure form, but are mixed with malignant elements, such as embryonal carcinoma.
  Choice E: CORRECT. The most common testicular tumor under the age of 3 is a yolk sac tumor (infantile embryonal carcinoma). However, finding any testicular tumor in children is uncommon. The prognosis is good in most cases.

QUESTION 33 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q33]
Category: Penile & Male Urethral Pathology | Subtopic: Penile & Male Urethral Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 43-year-old man has noted a lesion on his penis for the past year. On physical examination there is a 0.9 cm diameter rough, tan, firm, slightly raised area at the right lateral base of the glans. He is uncircumcised, and there is difficulty in retracting the foreskin. Which of the following is the most likely diagnosis?

CHOICES:
  A. Angiokeratoma
  B. Balanitis xerotica obliterans
  C. Bowen disease <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  D. Hard chancre
  E. Lichen simplex chronicus
  F. Paraphimosis

CORRECT ANSWER: C. Bowen disease

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  This is squamous cell carcinoma in situ. If excised at this stage, it is curable.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: C. Bowen disease

CORRECT. This is squamous cell carcinoma in situ. If excised at this stage, it is curable. If not treated, it can progress to an invasive carcinoma.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. This is a small type of hemangioma, which is most common on the surface of the scrotum.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Balanitis xerotica obliterans is the male equivalent of lichen sclerosus et atrophicus in the female, which white patches of atrophic epithelium and upper dermal chronic inflammation with fibrosis.
  Choice C: CORRECT. This is squamous cell carcinoma in situ. If excised at this stage, it is curable. If not treated, it can progress to an invasive carcinoma.
  Choice D: Incorrect. A hard chancre is a focal ulceration that results from primary syphilis infection.
  Choice E: Incorrect. Lichen simplex chronicus is epidermal hyperplasia and chronic inflammation that results from chronic rubbing and scratching, possibly secondary to a dermatophyte infection.
  Choice F: Incorrect. Paraphimosis occurs when phimotic foreskin becomes irreversibly retracted over the glans penis, resulting in edema of glans secondary to compromised venous/lymphatic return.

QUESTION 34 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q34]
Category: Testicular & Scrotal Pathology | Subtopic: Testicular & Scrotal Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 32-year-old has noted bilateral breast enlargement over the past 7 months. On physical examination there is bilateral gynecomastia without tenderness or masses. His external genitalia appear normal. An ultrasound scan reveals a 1 cm solid mass within the body of the right testis. Laboratory studies show a serum testosterone of 550 ng/dL and estradiol of 36 pg/mL. His serum alpha-fetoprotein is 9 ng/mL and HCG non-detectable. Which of the following neoplasms is this man most likely to have?

CHOICES:
  A. Choriocarcinoma
  B. Embryonal carcinoma
  C. Leydig cell tumor <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  D. Seminoma
  E. Yolk sac tumor

CORRECT ANSWER: C. Leydig cell tumor

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  A Leydig cell tumor is most often seen in adult men. It may produce androgens and/or estrogens, leading to gynecomastia and/or feminization.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: C. Leydig cell tumor

CORRECT. A Leydig cell tumor is most often seen in adult men. It may produce androgens and/or estrogens, leading to gynecomastia and/or feminization. It is histologically benign. Most are small in size.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Choriocarcinoma is a highly malignant form of germ cell neoplasm, typically producing significant amounts of HCG from the syncytial cells.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Embryonal carcinomas typically are associated with elevations in the alpha-fetoprotein. They tend to be large masses.
  Choice C: CORRECT. A Leydig cell tumor is most often seen in adult men. It may produce androgens and/or estrogens, leading to gynecomastia and/or feminization. It is histologically benign. Most are small in size.
  Choice D: Incorrect. Seminoma is the most common form of pure germ cell tumor of the testis. They may be associated with modest elevations of HCG. They do not produce estrogens or androgens.
  Choice E: Incorrect. The most common testicular tumor under the age of 3 is a yolk sac tumor (infantile embryonal carcinoma). There is typically an elevaed serum alpha-fetoprotein.

QUESTION 35 of 35 [ID: webpath-male-q35]
Category: Male Reproductive Pathology | Subtopic: Male Reproductive Pathology
----------------------------------------------------------------------
STEM:
A 35-year-old healthy man and his wife are unable to conceive for the past year, despite trying repeatedly for pregnancy. On physical examination his height is at the 80th percentile and weight at the 50th percentile for age. Palpation of his scrotum reveals that testes are small. Breasts are slightly enlarged. Laboratory studies show oligospermia. Which of the following is the most likely problem leading to infertility in this man?

CHOICES:
  A. Cachexia with poor nutrition
  B. Cryptorchidism
  C. Hypopituitarism
  D. Klinefelter syndrome <-- CORRECT ANSWER
  E. Repeated sexual intercourse

CORRECT ANSWER: D. Klinefelter syndrome

EDUCATIONAL OBJECTIVE / KEY TAKEAWAY:
  His height, gynecomastia, and infertility are features of Klinefelter syndrome. About 1 in 500 males has a 47, XXY karyotype.

OVERALL RATIONALE:
  Correct Answer: D. Klinefelter syndrome

CORRECT. His height, gynecomastia, and infertility are features of Klinefelter syndrome. About 1 in 500 males has a 47, XXY karyotype. Oligospermia does not preclude fertility; assisted reproduction techniques could be employed.

CHOICE-SPECIFIC EXPLANATIONS:
  Choice A: Incorrect. Weight at the 50th percentile is not cachexia. He is healthy.
  Choice B: Incorrect. Testes in the scrotum remove cryptorchidism from the list. A thorough physical exam is useful.
  Choice C: Incorrect. Hypopituitarism would leads to more than loss of FSH/LH, and he would not be healthy.
  Choice D: CORRECT. His height, gynecomastia, and infertility are features of Klinefelter syndrome. About 1 in 500 males has a 47, XXY karyotype. Oligospermia does not preclude fertility; assisted reproduction techniques could be employed.
  Choice E: Incorrect. One does not run out of the key components - spermatozoa and seminal fluid - from usage. Fertility in males can be preserved in old age.
